. Although this result is stated for stable complex orbifolds, our construction is in fact designed to derive a manifold from a normally complex orbifold. Since every step in the algorithm preserves the additional datum of the stable complex structure, working in the normally complex setting implies the desired result. In view of the applicability to concrete geometric problems from symplectic topology, we thus prefer to state our construction just using the normal complex structures.

ALGORITHM I: From orbifolds to manifolds

Input:
A compact normally complex orbifold
{\mathcal{Z}}
.

Step 1

Invoke Theorem
3.7
to construct a smooth map from a compact normally complex orbifold

(5.2)

\pi^{ab}:{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}\to{\mathcal{Z}}

such that
{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}
only has abelian stabilizers.

Step 2

Conduct the construction from Theorem
4.6
to the pair
({\mathcal{Z}}^{ab},\emptyset)
and forget the data of the divisors
\tilde{\mathbf{D}}
to obtain an effective and compact normally complex compact orbifold
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}
with a smooth map

(5.3)

\pi^{M}:\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}\to{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}

such that
|\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}|
is a smooth manifold.

The upshot is that we obtain a compact orbifold
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}
with a map
\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}\to{\mathcal{Z}}
such that
|\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}|
is a smooth manifold by applying the above two steps. ∎

We call the combination of the two steps above the
destackification algorithm
.

Remark 5.4
.

(1)

The input of the above algorithm has ambient orbifold possibly with boundary. As remarked before, although Theorem
3.7
and Theorem
4.6
are stated for (derived) orbifolds without boundary, the same constructions go through for (derived) orbifolds with boundary by observing that for local charts for the form
({\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}\times{\mathbb{R}}^{n-1})//\Gamma
, the group
\Gamma
acts trivially on the
{\mathbb{R}}_{\geq 0}
direction, and all the arguments only concern the directions
normal
to the fixed point loci.

(2)

The only auxiliary choices made in the destackification algorithm is the choice of flattened Riemannian metric in the definition of blowups. As discussed in Section
3.2
, this is a contractible choice, and the result of the blowup procedure is thus well-defined up to bordism.

We now prove our main result concerning the relationship between manifold and orbifold bordism:

Proof of Theorem
1.20
.

To show that Algorithm I is compatible with bordism, let
\mathcal{Z}
be a normally complex orbifold, which bounds a compact orbifold
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}
. Because of the compatibility of normal complex structures and the functoriality of open embedding as stated in Theorem
3.7
and Theorem
4.6
, we can use the collar embedding of the boundary to make sure that

(5.4)

\widetilde{\hat{\mathcal{Z}}}^{ab}

defines a null bordism of
|\tilde{\mathcal{Z}}^{ab}|
. Assuming that
\mathcal{Z}
is a normally complex orbifold with boundary, lying over a pair
(X,A)
, and that
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}
bounds over this pair, we obtain the desired natural transformation by composing the projection
\widetilde{\hat{\mathcal{Z}}}^{ab}\to\hat{\mathcal{Z}}
with the map to the target space.

To show compatibility with the module action, it suffices to prove that, if
M
s a manifold, then the resolution of singularities of
M\times\mathcal{Z}
is the product of
M
with the resolution of singularities of
\mathcal{Z}
. This follows by noting that every step in the Algorithm I, which involves taking blowups and root stacks, will have such a product decomposition.

∎

5.3.
Geometric bordisms

We begin with results concerning
\Gamma
-equivariant geometric bordism, for a finite group
\Gamma
, which assigns to a pair
(X,A)
of topological spaces the graded group
\Omega^{U,\Gamma}_{*}(X,A)
of bordism classes of maps of pairs
(M,\partial M)\to(X,A)
, where
M
is a compact stably complex
\Gamma
-manifold.

Proof of Theorem
1.3
.

Our goal is to construct a map

(5.5)

\Omega^{U,\Gamma}_{*}(X,A)\to\Omega^{U}_{*}(X,A),

which assigns to a free
\Gamma
-manifold its quotient by
\Gamma
. Appealing to Theorem
1.20
, we may define such a map as the composition of the inclusion of equivariant bordism in orbifold bordism which takes a
\Gamma
-manifold to the quotient orbifold, followed by the splitting we construct.
∎

Tracing through the construction shows that the map we produced assigns to a general
\Gamma
-manifold the result of applying the resolution of singularities algorithm to the associated orbifold quotient, consisting of the two steps of abelianization of the fixed point locus and desingularization of the associated quotient space. The former replaces the manifold
M
, by blow-ups along fixed point strata, with a
\Gamma
-manifold with the property that the representations associated to all fixed points are diagonalizable, following the proof of Theorem
3.7
, appearing at the end of Section
3.7
, and the latter procedure is the desingularization procedure from Theorem
4.6
. As both procedures are algorithmic, the construction is functorial, and compatible both with cobordisms and with boundary homomorphisms.

Remark 5.5
.

There is some flexibility in the properties satisfied by the spliting map. For example, we may alternately set it to vanish on all bordism classes represented by manifolds for which the isotropy at a generic point is non-trivial, and use resolution of singularities only on the component coming from bordism classes equipped with faithful actions. Alternatively, we may factor Equation (
5.5
) through a map to a direct sum of copies of
\Omega^{U}_{*}(X,A)
, indexed by conjugacy classes of normal sugroups in
\Gamma
, and which record the generic isotropy.

The next proof requires a small modification of the results of Section
3
and
4
, by imposing an additional condition of equivariance:

Proof of Theorem
1.12
.

Given a surjection
\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
with kernel
K
, and pair
(Y,B)
of
\Gamma^{\prime}
spaces, we construct a map

(5.6)

\Omega^{U,\Gamma}_{*}(Y,B)\to\Omega^{U,\Gamma^{\prime}}_{*}(Y,B).

Given a
\Gamma
-manifold
M
, we consider the quotient orbifold
M//K
, which lies over
(Y,B)
and carries a residual
\Gamma^{\prime}
action. We then apply the constructions of of Section
3
and
4
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
-equivariantly, to obtain a manifold over the pair
(Y,B)
: equivariance can be achieved because the only choice is that of a flattening of the almost complex structure near the fixed point strata, which can be done
\Gamma
-equivariantly on
M
, hence descends to a
\Gamma^{\prime}
-equivariant choice on
M//K
.
∎

6.
Functorial embedded resolution of singularities and universal zero loci

6.1.
Abramovich–Temkin–Wlodarczyk’s functorial resolution

We recall the functorial embedded resolution of singularities in characteristic
0
developed in
[
ATW19
]
. This approach is well-adapted to our local-to-global construction in Section
7
, though earlier results on functorial resolution of singularities, e.g.,
[
BM97
,
Wło05
,
Kol07
]
, may also suffice for our purpose. Throughout our discussion, we work over
{\mathbb{C}}
, although the results from
[
ATW19
]
are stated for any field of characteristic
0
.

Definition 6.1
.

The
category of pairs with smooth morphisms
over
{\mathbb{C}}
is given by the following data:

•

the objects are
pairs

(X\subset Y)
for which
Y
is a smooth variety over
{\mathbb{C}}
, and
X
is a reduced closed subscheme of
Y
;

•

given two pairs
(X_{1}\subset Y_{1})
and
(X_{2}\subset Y_{2})
, a morphism
(X_{1}\subset Y_{1})\to(X_{2}\subset Y_{2})
is given by a smooth morphism
Y_{1}\to Y_{2}
so that
X_{1}=X_{2}\times_{Y_{2}}Y_{1}
is the pullback of
X_{2}
.

Theorem 6.2

(
[
ATW19
, Theorem 1.1.1]
)
.

There is an endofunctor
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
of the category of pairs with smooth morphisms over
{\mathbb{C}}
whose image consists of pairs
(X\subset Y)
satisfying the property that
X
is a smooth closed subscheme.

More precisely, the above statement is a corollary of
[
ATW19
, Theorem 1.1.1]
, so it deserves some further comments.

(1)

The actual output of
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
as stated in
[
ATW19
, Theorem 1.1.1]
is a pair of smooth
Deligne–Mumford stacks
. One needs to appeal to Bergh’s destackification theorem
[
BR19
]
,
[
ATW19
, Theorem 8.1.2]
to obtain the statement involving only schemes, see the discussion in
[
ATW19
, Section 1.5]
. In other words, our notation
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
is in fact a composition of
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
as in
[
ATW19
, Theorem 1.1.1]
and Bergh’s destackification functor.

(2)

Before applying the destackification functor, the functor
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
is obtained as the stabilization of an endofunctor
F_{er}
defined on the category of pairs with smooth
surjective
morphisms. The functor
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
is constructed via
weighted blow-ups
, and strictly reduces an invariant
\mathrm{maxinv}(X)
associated to a pair
X\subset Y
, which measures the “worst” singularities of
X
via intersecting
X
with hypersurfaces in
Y
.

The following statement is a straightforward consequence of the functoriality property.

Corollary 6.3
.

Let
(X_{1}\subset Y_{1})\to(X_{2}\subset Y_{2})
be a smooth morphisms between pairs. For
i=1,2
, define
(\tilde{X}_{i},\tilde{Y}_{i}):=F^{\circ\infty}_{er}(X_{i}\subset Y_{i})
. Then the diagram

(6.1)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{X_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Y_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{X}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{Y}_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces X_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Y_{2}}

is commumative. Moreover, the top and bottom faces are smooth morphisms of pairs, and the vertical arrows are compositions of destackifications and weighted blow-ups. ∎

Functorial resolutions of singularities interact perfectly with group actions. The next statement is well-known, see, e.g.,
[
Kol07
, Proposition 3.9.1]
. We include a proof for completeness.

Corollary 6.4
.

Given a pair
(X\subset Y)
, write
(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})\subset F^{\circ\infty}_{er}(X\subset Y)
. If an algebraic group
G
acts on
Y
, preserving
X
, then there is a unique
G
action on
\tilde{Y}
so that the projection
\tilde{Y}\to Y
is equivariant. Such an action necessarily preserves
\tilde{X}
.

Proof.

The action of
G
on the pair
(X\subset Y)
is given by a smooth morphism

(6.2)

m:G\times(X\subset Y)=(G\times X\subset G\times Y)\to(X\subset Y).

By functoriality, the pair
(\widetilde{G\times X},\widetilde{G\times Y}):=F^{\circ\infty}_{er}(G\times X\subset G\times Y)
is given by the pull-back of
(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y}):=F^{\circ\infty}_{er}(X\subset Y)
via the action
m
.

On the other hand, the projection
\pi_{2}:G\times(X\subset Y)\to(X\subset Y)
is smooth. Using the functoriality again, we see that
(\widetilde{G\times X},\widetilde{G\times Y})=(G\times\tilde{X},G\times\tilde{Y})
. Accordingly, we get a commutative diagram

(6.3)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G\times\tilde{X}\subset G\times\tilde{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\widetilde{G\times X}\subset\widetilde{G\times Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G\times X\subset G\times Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(G\times X\subset G\times Y)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(X\subset Y)}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cong}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces=}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m}

Here the morphism of pairs
m^{\prime}:(\widetilde{G\times X},\widetilde{G\times Y})\to(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})
is induced by the functor
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
. We define the morphism

(6.4)

\tilde{m}:G\times(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})\to(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})

to be the composition of the top arrow.

We claim that
\tilde{m}
defines a
G
-action on the pair
(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})
. In other words, we need to verify the commutativity of the diagram

(6.5)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times G\times(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces G\times(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces m_{G}\times id}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces id_{G}\times\tilde{m}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{m}}

The point is, as
G
acts on the pair
(X\subset Y)
, and the resolution
(\tilde{X}\subset\tilde{Y})\to(X\subset Y)
is birational, we know that commutativity holds over a dense open set. The desired commutativity is a result of the separatedness and reducedness of all the schemes in our discussion.
∎

We will use actions of both finite groups and general reductive groups in our later discussions.

For the proof of an important technical statement (Lemma
6.16
), we need a counterpart of Theorem
6.2
in the complex analytic setting.

Definition 6.5
.

The
category of complex analytic pairs with smooth morphisms
is given by the following data:

•

the objects are
pairs

(X\subset Y)
for which
Y
is a smooth complex manifold, and
X
is a reduced closed complex analytic space of
Y
;

•

a morphism
(X_{1}\subset Y_{1})\to(X_{2}\subset Y_{2})
is given by a holomorphic map
Y_{1}\to Y_{2}
which is a submersion (i.e., locally modeled on the projection map
Y_{2}\times{\mathbb{C}}^{r}\to Y_{2}
) and so that
X_{1}=X_{2}\times_{Y_{2}}Y_{1}
is the pullback of
X_{2}
.

Theorem 6.6

(
[
Wło24
]
)
.

There is an endofunctor
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
of category of complex analytic pairs with smooth morphisms whose image consists of pairs
(X\subset Y)
satisfying the property that
X
is a complex submanifold. This endofunctor is compatible with the corresponding algebraic functor in the sense that, for an algebraic pair
(X\subset Y)
, the analytification of
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}(X\subset Y)
is naturally isomorphic to the result of applying
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
to the analytic pair underlying
(X\subset Y)
.

Sketch of proof.

We list the important technical steps in
[
ATW19
]
and comment on how to adapt the arguments to the complex analytic setting.

(1)

As in the algebraic setting, the final resolution functor
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
is obtained as the composition of Bergh’s destackification and the stabilization of the counterpart of the endofunctor
F_{er}
in the complex analytic setting. Bergh’s destackification procedure has been reproduced in Section
3
and Section
4
in the almost complex setting, which can be modified to work in the complex analytic setting by combining with the original reference
[
Ber17
,
BR19
]
.

(2)

The pair
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}(X\subset Y)
is obtained, as in the algebraic case, by applying the weighted blow-up to
Y
along the center specified by the locus where the function
\mathrm{inv}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{X})
(cf.
[
ATW19
, Section 5.1]
) achieves its maximum. Here
{\mathcal{I}}_{X}\subsetneq{\mathcal{O}}_{Y}
is the ideal associated with
X
. The function
\mathrm{inv}_{p}({\mathcal{I}}_{X})
is constructed inductively by looking at the hypersurface of maximal contact (cf.
[
ATW19
, Section 4.2]
) and the restricted coefficient ideals (cf.
[
ATW19
, Section 4.1, 4.3, 5.1]
). Since the uniqueness of maximal contact hypersurfaces up to complex analytic isomorphism holds, and the arguments concerning coefficient ideals work in the same way for the rings of complex analytic functions, the center for the desired weighted blow-up is uniquely specified (cf.
[
ATW19
, Theorem 5.3.1, Theorem 6.1.1]
). It follows from the local constructions that the centers from the algebraic arguments and complex analytic arguments agree.

(3)

The final piece of this discussion is about weighted blow-ups. This is done in
[
ATW19
, Section 3]
via a Rees algebra construction, but the equivalent constructions via local defining equation or toric models (cf.
[
ATW19
, Section 3.5, 3.6]
) show that the same can be done in the complex analytic setting, working with complex orbifolds.

Therefore, the functor
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
can be defined via the above procedures. The desired functoriality follows from the functoriality of
\mathrm{inv}_{p}
under smooth submersions (the conterpart of smooth morphisms in the complex analytic setting) (cf.
[
ATW19
, Theorem 5.1.1]
). The coincidence of
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
and
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
on algebraic pairs holds because each step of the algorithm matches.
∎

6.2.
Resolving universal zero loci

We revisit the crucial algebraic varieties
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
constructed from the universal zero locus of equivariant polynomial maps between complex
\Gamma
-representations
V
and
W
where
\Gamma
is a finite group. The canonical equivariant Whitney stratifications on
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
was studied extensively in
[
BX22b
, Section 4]
, but we study them instead through the lens of resolution of singularities.

Definition 6.7
.

Let
\Gamma
be a finite group. Suppose that
V
and
W
are finite-dimensional complex
\Gamma
-representations. For a positive integer
d
, recall that
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
is the affine space of
\Gamma
-equivariant polynomial maps of degree at most
d
. Define the
universal zero locus
to be the
0
-locus of the evaluation map from
V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
to
W
:

(6.6)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W):={\rm ev}^{-1}(0)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\{0\}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces W}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\rm ev}}

The product
V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
admits a
\Gamma
-action which acts on
V
by the natural action and acts trivally on the second factor
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
. This action preserves the universal zero locus
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
. The proof of the following fact can be found at
[
BX22b
, Proposition 4.9]
.

Lemma 6.8

(Cf.
[
FO97
, Lemma 5]
)
.

If
V
is a faithful
\Gamma
-representation, there exists an integer
d_{0}\geq 1
such that for any
d\geq d_{0}
, the subvariety

(6.7)

Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}:=\{(v,P)\in Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)\ |\ \text{the stabilizer of }(v,P)\text{ under the }\Gamma\text{-action is trivial}\}

lies in the regular locus of the evaluation map
{\rm ev}
. In particular, it is smooth of dimension
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)+\dim_{\mathbb{C}}V-\dim_{\mathbb{C}}W
. ∎

Remark 6.9
.

The above transversality result is eventually reduced to the following basic fact about the representation theory of finite groups: under the same assumption, there exists
d_{0}
such that
W
is included as a subrepresentation of the symmetric product
\mathrm{Sym}^{d_{0}}(V)
with the induced
\Gamma
-action. The faithfulness of
V
is crucial here.

We will use Lemma
6.8
to make sure that the resolution of the zero locus of the section is an orbifold which has the expected dimension.

Denote by
\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
the Zariski closure of
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
in
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
. By construction,
\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
is preserved under the
\Gamma
-action on
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
. Accordingly,

(6.8)

(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\subset V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W))

is pair over
{\mathbb{C}}
acted on by the finite group
\Gamma
.

Definition 6.10
.

Define
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
to be the resolution of
\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
by applying the functor
F_{er}^{\circ\infty}
from Theorem
6.2
to the
\Gamma
-equivariant pair
\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\subset V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
, which comes with a
\Gamma
-equivariant morphism

(6.9)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\to V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W).

Next, we prove that when varying the cut-off degree
d
, the resolutions
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
are compatible with each other in the following sense.

Proposition 6.11
.

Given a sufficiently large integer
d\geq 1
and any integer
d^{\prime}\geq d
, the morphisms of Equation (
6.9
) fit into a
\Gamma
-equivariant Cartesian commutative diagram

(6.10)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d^{\prime}}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d^{\prime}}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}

where the right vertical arrow is the natural inclusion.

Note that the morphism
V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)\to V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d^{\prime}}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
is
not
smooth if
d^{\prime}>d
. Therefore, Proposition
6.11
does not follow directly from the functoriality part of Theorem
6.2
. To establish the commutativity of Diagram (
6.10
), we need to appeal to the following statement:

Proposition 6.12

(Cf.
[
BX22b
, Lemma 4.14]
)
.

Denote by
\phi_{d}:V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)\to V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
the natural inclusion map. Then there exists an integer
d_{0}\geq 1
, which depends on
\Gamma,V
and
W
such that for any
d\geq d_{0}
, there exists a morphism

(6.11)

\underline{\phi}_{d}^{\prime}:V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)\to\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)

so that the induced morphism

(6.12)

\displaystyle\phi_{d}^{\prime}:V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)

\displaystyle\to V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)

\displaystyle(v,P)

\displaystyle\mapsto(v,\underline{\phi}_{d}^{\prime}(v,P))

satisfies the following properties.

(1)

The composition
\phi_{d}^{\prime}\circ\phi_{d}
is equal to the identity map on
V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
.

(2)

The equality of evaluation

(6.13)

P(v)=\underline{\phi}_{d}^{\prime}(v,P)(v)

holds for any pair
(v,P)\in V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
.

Proof.

We reproduce the proof here because it will be needed for the following discussions.

Because the space of
\Gamma
-equivariant polynomial maps of all degrees
\mathrm{Poly}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
is a finitely generated module over the ring of
\Gamma
-invariant functions
{\mathbb{C}}[V]^{\Gamma}
, we can choose generators
Q_{1},\dots,Q_{n}
. Let
d_{0}
be the maximum of the degrees of
Q_{1},\dots,Q_{n}
. Then for any
d\geq d_{0}
, let
\{P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\}
be a complex basis of homogeneous
\Gamma
-equivariant polynomial maps from
V
to
W
of degree
(d+1)
. Then we can find
h_{ij}\in{\mathbb{C}}[V]^{\Gamma}
for
1\leq i\leq k,1\leq j\leq n
such that

(6.14)

P_{i}=\sum_{j}h_{ij}Q_{j}.

Using these choices, we can define

(6.15)

\displaystyle\phi_{d}^{\prime}:V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)

\displaystyle\to V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)

\displaystyle(v,P)

\displaystyle\mapsto(v,P-P^{\prime}+\sum_{i,j}a_{i}h_{ij}(v)Q_{j}),

where
P^{\prime}
is the degree
d+1
part of
P
which can be written as
P^{\prime}=\sum_{i}a_{i}P_{i}
using the basis
\{P_{1},\dots,P_{k}\}
. This map satisfies the desired properties.
∎

Corollary 6.13
.

The morphism
\phi_{d}^{\prime}
from Equation (
6.12
) is a smooth morphism. Moreover, there exists a
\Gamma
-equivariant Cartesian diagram

(6.16)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}.}

Proof.

By Proposition
6.12
, the morphism
\phi_{d}^{\prime}
and its induced maps between Kähler differentials at each point admit a left inverse defined by modifying (
6.12
) as follows. Given an element
(v,\tilde{P})\in V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
, we denote the degree
(d+1)
part of
\tilde{P}
by
\tilde{P}^{\prime}
, and write it as
\tilde{P}^{\prime}=\sum_{i}\tilde{a}_{i}P_{i}
using the basis from the proof of Proposition
6.12
. Note that the image of
(v,\tilde{P})
under
\phi_{d}^{\prime}
is
(v,\tilde{P}-\tilde{P}^{\prime}+\sum_{i,j}\tilde{a}_{i}h_{ij}(v)Q_{j})
. We produce a right inverse of
\phi_{d}^{\prime}
around this point by

(6.17)

V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)\ni(v,P)\mapsto(v,P+\tilde{P}^{\prime}-\sum_{i,j}\tilde{a}_{i}h_{ij}(v)Q_{j}).

Because the composition of this map with
\phi_{d}^{\prime}
on the right is the identity, we see that the induced map of
\phi_{d}^{\prime}
on Kähler differentials admits a left inverse at
(v,\tilde{P})
.

By the implicit function theorem
[
Bos13
, Chapter 8.5, Proposition 10]
, we conclude that
\phi_{d}^{\prime}
is smooth since the source and target of this map are both smooth. By item (2) of Proposition
6.12
, we know that we have a Cartesian diagram

(6.18)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{Z}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\phi_{d}^{\prime}}

such that all the arrows are
\Gamma
-equivariant, and it defines a smooth morphism of pairs

(6.19)

(\overline{Z}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\subset V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W))\to(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\subset V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)).

As a result, we can apply the resolution algorithm Theorem
6.2
and Corollary
6.3
to obtain Diagram (
6.16
). The
\Gamma
-equivariance follows from Corollary
6.4
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.11
.

We consider the (
\Gamma
-equivariant) scheme
Z
defined as the fiber product

(6.20)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}

where the bottom horizontal arrow is the composition of the blow-down map
\tilde{Z}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\to\overline{Z}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
and the inclusion map
\overline{Z}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\to V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
. It can be concatenated with Diagram (
6.16
), which gives us the commutative diagram

(6.21)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+1}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W).}}

Because the two “inner” squares are Cartesian, we see that the “outer” square

(6.22)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{Z}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}

is also Cartesian. Now we observe that the bottom arrow is the identity map by Proposition
6.12
, which implies that the top arrow is an isomorphism. This in turn establishes Proposition
6.11
for the case
d^{\prime}=1
. For general
d^{\prime}
, we just need to concatenate diagrams of the form (
6.10
).
∎

We are also interested in restricted representations. Let
\Gamma_{1}
be a finite group and let
V
and
W
be finite-dimensional
\Gamma_{1}
-representations. Suppose that
\Gamma_{2}<\Gamma_{1}
is a proper subgroup. By restricting the representations along
\Gamma_{2}
, we obtain a decomposition

(6.23)

V=\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}\oplus\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}},

where
\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}
is the direct sum of all
\Gamma_{2}
-trivial irreducible subrepresentations of
V
. Introduce the following notation

(6.24)

V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}:=\{v\in V\ |\text{ the stabilizer of }v\text{ is a subgroup of }\Gamma_{2}\}.

Consider
V
and the product
\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times W
as the total spaces of
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant vector bundles over the affine space
\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}
equipped with the trivial
\Gamma_{2}
-action

(6.25)

\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}=\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}},\quad\quad\quad\underline{W}=\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times W.

Any element
\check{P}\in\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}\oplus\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)
can be viewed as a
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant bundle morphism
\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\to\underline{W}
which over for any
\mathring{v}\in\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}
is given by
\check{P}(\mathring{v},-)\in\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)
. Write

(6.26)

\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})

the bundle of
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant fiberwise polynomial maps
\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\to\underline{W}
of degree at most
d
.

Now given
P\in\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)
, the natural inclusion
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)\to\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)
by viewing a
\Gamma_{1}
-equivariant polynomial map as a
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant polynomial map defines a morphism

(6.27)

\displaystyle V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)

\displaystyle\to\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})

\displaystyle(v,P)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big((\mathring{v},\check{v}),P(\mathring{v},-)\big),

where for any
v\in V
, we use the decomposition in Equation (
6.23
) to write
v=(\mathring{v},\check{v})
.

After this preliminary discussion, we can start investigating the universal zero loci. Continuing the above setting, define

(6.28)

\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}\subset\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W}),

to be the Zariski closure of the subset of the fiberwise zero locus consisting of points with trivial stabilizer under the
\Gamma_{2}
-action, which should be thought of as a family version of (
6.8
). By construction, we have a Cartesian diagram

(6.29)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})}

Remark 6.14
.

We wish to show that by applying the functor
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
to the pairs defined by the horizontal arrows of the above diagram, we can lift the Cartesian diagram to the resolutions. Unfortunately, we do not know a proof within the algebraic category, and we prove the desired result in the complex analytic setting. Nevertheless, we expect that the Cartesian square (
6.31
) holds in the algebraic category.

Write
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}
the resolution of
\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}
by applying
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
from Theorem
6.6
to the pair
(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}\subset\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W}))
, and it admits a morphism

(6.30)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}\to\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W}).

Here we abuse the notations because after passing to the underlying complex manifolds, the outputs of
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
and
F^{\circ\infty}_{er}
agree in view of Theorem
6.6
.

Proposition 6.15
.

Denote by
\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}}
the open subvariety of
\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}
as in Equation (
6.24
). For
d
sufficiently large, after restricting to
V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}
, the analogue of the morphism (
6.9
) from
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
and the morphism (
6.30
) fit into a
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant Cartesian commutative diagram

(6.31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W}).}}

where the right vertical arrow is Equation (
6.27
).

We need some preparation to prove the above Proposition.

Lemma 6.16
.

For
d
sufficiently large, near any point
(v,P)\in\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)^{\circ}\cap V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)
, the natural inclusion map

(6.32)

V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)\subset V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)

has a complex analytic left inverse
\theta_{d}
which preserves the evaluation map
(v,P)\mapsto P(v)
.

Proof.

The space
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)
is a finite-dimensional complex
\Gamma_{1}
-representation under the conjugation action whose trivial isotypic piece is identified with
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)
.. Therefore, given any
P\in\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)
, it admits a canonical decomposition
P=P^{\prime}+P^{\prime\prime}
where
P^{\prime}
is
\Gamma_{1}
-equivariant.

By the Lagrange interpolation method, there exists an integer
d_{0}
such that for any
v\in V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}
, there is a
\Gamma_{2}
-invariant polynomial
L_{v}:V\to{\mathbb{C}}
of degree at most
d_{0}
which satisfies
L_{v}(\gamma v)=1
for
\gamma\in\Gamma_{2}
and
L_{v}(\gamma v)=0
for
\gamma\in\Gamma_{1}\setminus\Gamma_{2}
. Locally,
L_{v}
depends complex analytically on
v
. Indeed, fixing
v_{0}
, one may choose a
1
-dimensional subspace
l_{v_{0}}\subset V
and a complex linear projection from
V
to
l_{v_{0}}
such that the
\Gamma_{1}
orbits of
v_{0}
have distinct images. This property holds for all
v
sufficiently close to
v_{0}
. Then
L_{v}
is obtained by composing the standard
1
-dimensional Lagrangian interpolation polynomial with this linear transformation followed by taking average over
\Gamma_{2}
. We define a map
V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)\to V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d+d_{0}}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)
by

(6.33)

(v,P)\mapsto(v,P^{\prime}+\frac{1}{|\Gamma_{2}|}\sum_{\gamma\in\Gamma_{1}}\gamma^{-1}\circ(L_{v}\cdot P^{\prime\prime})\circ\gamma).

Note that this map may increase the degree by
d_{0}
, but we can compose it with the map
\phi_{d}^{\prime}
from Equation (
6.12
) to bring the degree back in range. We denote such a map by
\theta_{d}
, and one can check from the definition that it satisfies the desired properties.
∎

Lemma 6.17
.

By applying
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
to the pairs
(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)^{\circ}\subset V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W))
and
(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)^{\circ}\subset V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W))
, the morphisms from Equaion (
6.9
) fit into a
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant Cartesian diagram

(6.34)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)}}

Proof.

By Lemma
6.16
, locally near any given
(v,P)\in V_{+}^{\Gamma_{1}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)\subset V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)
, there exists a complex analytic submersion
V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)\to V_{+}^{\Gamma_{1}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)
which preserves the evaluation map. Then the Cartesian-ness of Diagram (
6.34
) holds locally as in the proof of Proposition
6.11
. Using the functoriality again, we see that these local diagrams can be patched together to give the desired result.
∎

Proof of Proposition
6.15
.

Consider the following map

(6.35)

\displaystyle V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)

\displaystyle\to\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})

\displaystyle(v,P)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big((\mathring{v},\check{v}),P(\mathring{v},-)\big)

as in Equation (
6.27
). By inspecting the definition, this is a complex analytic submersion (in fact, an algebraic one) such that the evaluation map is preserved. Therefore, we obtain a smooth morphism of complex analytic pairs

(6.36)

(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)^{\circ}\subset V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W))\to(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}\subset\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})).

Applying the resolution functor
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
, we obtain a Cartesian diagram

(6.37)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W}).}}

By concatenating this diagram with (
6.34
), we see that (
6.31
) is Cartesian. The
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariance follows from funtoriality.
∎

The above Proposition provides the key property which eventually permits the possibility of the local-to-global approach to the resolution of singularities. This replaces the role of the canonical Whitney stratification as developed in
[
BX22b
, Section 4]
.

We need a result concerning the behavior of zero-loci under stabilizations. Let
\Gamma
be a finite group, and suppose that
V
,
W
, and
V^{\prime}
are finite-dimensional complex
\Gamma
-representations such that
V
is a faithful
\Gamma
-representation. Then we can define a
\Gamma
-equivariant morphism

(6.38)

\displaystyle V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)

\displaystyle\to(V\oplus V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})

\displaystyle(v,P)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big((v,0),\mathrm{diag}(P,\mathrm{Id})\big),

where
\mathrm{Id}:V^{\prime}\to V^{\prime}
is the identity matrix, thought of as a
\Gamma
-equivariant polynomial map of degree
1
. This induces a morphism of pairs

(6.39)

\displaystyle\big(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}

\displaystyle\subset V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)\big)\to

\displaystyle\big(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})^{\circ}

\displaystyle\subset(V\oplus V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})\big).

The following statement asserts the compatibility of resolutions under such stabilization.

Proposition 6.18
.

We have a
\Gamma
-equivariant Cartesian commutative diagram

(6.40)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V\oplus V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})},}

in which the horizontal arrows are the outcomes of Theorem
6.2
composed with the blow-down maps.

Proof.

We shall use the following description of the zero-locus as a fiber product:

(6.41)

Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})=Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)\times_{V\oplus V^{\prime}}Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},V^{\prime}).

For an element
(v,v^{\prime})\in V\oplus V^{\prime}
, note that if either
v
or
v^{\prime}
has trivial stabilizer, then
(v,v^{\prime})
has trivial stabilizer under the diagonal
\Gamma
-action. On the other hand, note that the variety
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},V^{\prime})
is smooth near the point
((v,0),\mathrm{Id}_{V^{\prime}})
for any
v\in V
, where the identity matrix
\mathrm{Id}_{V^{\prime}}
is treated as a
\Gamma
-equivariant polynomial map of degree
1
from
V\oplus V^{\prime}
to
V^{\prime}
which is independent of
v
. As a result, for the subvariety
\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)^{\circ}\times_{V\oplus V^{\prime}}Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},V^{\prime})
, near
V\times\{0\}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)\times\{\mathrm{Id}_{V^{\prime}}\}
, the resolution can be identified with

(6.42)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)^{\circ}\times_{V\oplus V^{\prime}}Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},V^{\prime}).

Furthermore, we have an inclusion
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)\to Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)
defined by
(v,P)\mapsto((v,0),P)
, where
P\in\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
is viewed as an element of
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)
which is independent of
V^{\prime}
. We claim that we have a Cartesian diagram

(6.43)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V\oplus V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)}.}

Indeed, we can construct a left inverse of the right vertical arrow by the formula

(6.44)

((v,v^{\prime}),P)\mapsto(v,P(-,v^{\prime}))

which preserves the evaluation map. It defines a surjective smooth morphism of pairs

(6.45)

(\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)^{\circ}\subset(V\oplus V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W))\to(\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\subset V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)),

so we can again argue as in the proof of Proposition
6.11
to show that we can construct Diagram (
6.43
). Then we concatenate Diagram (
6.43
) with the following diagram

(6.46)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V\oplus V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W)^{\circ}\times_{V\oplus V^{\prime}}Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},V^{\prime})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V\oplus V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})}.}

By the smoothness of
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},V^{\prime})
near
((v,0),\mathrm{Id}_{V^{\prime}})
, we know the bottom horizontal arrow is identified with

(6.47)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})^{\circ}\to(V\oplus V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})

near
((v,0),\mathrm{Id}_{V^{\prime}})
. Therefore, such a concatenation finishes the proof.
∎

Finally, we discuss the following situation which will be used to show that the splitting maps in Theorem
1.7
and Theorem
1.21
are module map. Slightly differently from the above setting, let
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
be finite groups, and suppose that
V_{1}
and
W_{1}
are finite-dimensional complex
\Gamma_{1}
-representations such that
V_{1}
is faithful, while
V_{2}
is a finite-dimensional complex
\Gamma_{2}
-representation. Then there exists a
\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant morphism

(6.48)

\displaystyle(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})

\displaystyle\to(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})

\displaystyle\big((v_{1},v_{2}),P_{1})\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big((v_{1},v_{2}),P_{1}\big),

where we declare that the
\Gamma_{2}
-action on
W_{1}
is trivial, and we view
P_{1}\in\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})
which does not depend on the
V_{2}
-factor. It induces a morphism of pairs

(6.49)

\displaystyle\big(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})^{\circ}\times V_{2}

\displaystyle\subset(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})\big)\to

\displaystyle\big(\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})^{\circ}

\displaystyle\subset(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})\big).

Proposition 6.19
.

The morphism (
6.49
) lifts to a
\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant Cartesian commutative diagram

(6.50)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})^{\circ}\times V_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1}),}}

in which the horizontal arrows are the outcomes of Theorem
6.2
composed with the blow-down maps.

Proof.

Consider the following
\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant morphism:

(6.51)

\displaystyle(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})

\displaystyle\to(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})

\displaystyle\big((v_{1},v_{2}),P_{2}\big)

\displaystyle\mapsto\big((v_{1},v_{2}),P_{2}(-,v_{2})\big).

This is a smooth morphism such that the universal zero loci are preserved. Then we have the commutative diagram

(6.52)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})^{\circ}\times V_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})^{\circ}\times V_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})}}

where the upper square comes from (
6.49
) and the lower square is induced from the aforementioned smooth morphism. By construction, the outer square has vertical arrows given by the identity map. Then applying the functoriality aspect of Theorem
6.2
to the lower square and the outer square, as in the proof of Proposition
6.11
, we conclude the desired result on the compatibility of the resolutions of singularities.
∎

6.3.
Smooth bundles of algebraic varieties

We need to introduce a notion of
smooth
families of
algebraic varieties
. This is of course a standard notion when the varieties are smooth, but we need a generalization to the singular case, which is less commonly considered. .

Definition 6.20
.

Let
X
be a smooth algebraic variety over
{\mathbb{C}}
. Given a smooth manifold
M
, an
X
-bundle
over
M
is a smooth fiber bundle with fibre
X
, together with a reduction of the structure group from
\mathrm{Diff}(X)
to the
algebraic automorphism group

\mathrm{Aut}(X)
of the variety
X
.

As usual, such a structure may be presented by an open cover
\{U_{\alpha}\}
, and transition functions

(6.53)

g_{\alpha\beta}:U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\to\mathrm{Aut}(X)

for every pairs of elements of this cover, which satisfy the cocycle cocycle condition
g_{\alpha\beta}\cdot g_{\beta\gamma}=g_{\alpha\gamma}
over each triple intersection
U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\cap U_{\gamma}
, such that
g_{\alpha\alpha}
is the identity.

For example, a complex vector bundle
E\to M
of rank
k
defines a
{\mathbb{C}}^{k}
-bundle over
M
in the above sense. Our primary example is the following:

Lemma 6.21
.

Suppose
\Gamma
is a finite group. Let
M
be a connected smooth manifold which is viewed as a
\Gamma
-manifold with the trivial
\Gamma
-action. Let
V
and
W
be
\Gamma
-representations, with
\Gamma
assumed to be faithful, and let
\mathbf{V}
and
\mathbf{W}
be
\Gamma
-equivariant vector bundles over
M
with fibers respectively isomorphic to
V
and
W
. For a sufficiently large
d\geq 1
, let
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
be the smooth
\Gamma
-variety from Definition
6.10
. Then there exists a
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
-bundle over
M

(6.54)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})^{\circ}\to M

admitting a
\Gamma
-equivariant bundle map to

(6.55)

\mathbf{V}\times_{M}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W}),

where the later denotes the
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
-bundle over
M
induced from the vector bundle structure.

Proof.

Suppose
\{U_{\alpha}\}
is an open cover of
M
such that over any
U_{\alpha}
, the fiber product
\mathbf{V}\times_{M}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})
is isomorphic to the product
U_{\alpha}\times V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
. Furthermore, we observe that
\mathbf{V}\times_{M}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})
can be reconstructed from these local pieces from smooth transition maps

(6.56)

g_{\alpha\beta}:U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\to GL(V)^{\Gamma}\times GL(W)^{\Gamma}.

Indeed, given
(v,P)\in GL(V)^{\Gamma}\times GL(W)^{\Gamma}
and
(g_{1},g_{2})\in GL(V)^{\Gamma}\times GL(W)^{\Gamma}
, the latter takes the former to
(g_{1}v,g_{2}P(g_{1}^{-1}v))
.
Observe that the pair

(6.57)

\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\subset V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)

from (
6.8
) is preserved under the
GL(V)^{\Gamma}\times GL(W)^{\Gamma}
-action. By Corollary
6.4
, the algebraic group
GL(V)^{\Gamma}\times GL(W)^{\Gamma}
acts on the resolution
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
as automorphisms, and the morphism
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}\to V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
is both
GL(V)^{\Gamma}\times GL(W)^{\Gamma}
-equivariant and
\Gamma
-equivariant. Accordingly, the transition maps
g_{\alpha\beta}
define

(6.58)

g_{\alpha}:U_{\alpha}\cap U_{\beta}\to GL(V)^{\Gamma}\times GL(W)^{\Gamma}\to\mathrm{Aut}(\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})^{\circ})

and they satisfy the cocycle condition by construction. The
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
-bundle
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})^{\circ}\to M
is constructed by globalizing, and the
\Gamma
-equivariant bundle map to
\mathbf{V}\times_{M}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})
is just the fiberwise blow-down map composed with the inclusion.
∎

The total space
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})^{\circ}
is acted on by
\Gamma
and it preserves the fibration structure over
M
. The
\Gamma
-equivariant complex structure on
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
defines a normal complex structure on the
\Gamma
-manifold
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})^{\circ}
in the sense of Definition
2.8
. We will use bundles of the form
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})^{\circ}
to define a suitable version of equivariant transversality in the next section and resolve the singularities of certain orbispaces inside normally complex orbifolds.

6.4.
Zero loci with prescribed stabilizer

In the above discussions, we studied the resolution of singularities of
\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
the compactification of the isotropy-free part of the universal zero locus. Following
[
BX22b
]
, we study pieces of
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
with prescribed isotropy groups.

Let
\Gamma
,
V
, and
W
be the same as in Definition
6.7
. Let
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma
be a subgroup. Define

(6.59)

Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}:=\{(v,P)\in Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)\ |\ \text{the stabilizer of }(v,P)\text{ under the }\Gamma\text{-action is }\Gamma^{\prime}\}.

Lemma 6.22

(Cf.
[
BX22b
, Proposition 4.9]
)
.

If
V
is a faithful
\Gamma
-representation, then for
d
sufficiently large,
Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
is a smooth complex algebraic variety of dimension
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)+\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathring{V}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}-\dim_{\mathbb{C}}\mathring{W}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
, where
\mathring{V}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
and
\mathring{W}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
are respectively the trivial isotypic piece of
V
and
W
under the restricted
\Gamma^{\prime}
-action. ∎

Denote by
\gamma^{\prime}
the set of subgroups of
\Gamma
which are conjugate to
\Gamma^{\prime}
. Then the variety

(6.60)

Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}:=\bigcup_{\Gamma^{\prime}\in\gamma^{\prime}}Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}=\coprod_{\Gamma^{\prime}\in\gamma^{\prime}}Z_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}

is invariant under the
\Gamma
-action, and it is smooth provided that
d
is large enough. We always take such a
d
for subsequent discussions. Following Definition
6.10
, write
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
the resolution of the Zariski closure
\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
by applying the functor
F_{er}^{\circ\infty}
from Theorem
6.2
to the
\Gamma
-equivariant pair
\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\subset V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)
. Then we have a
\Gamma
-equivariant morphism

(6.61)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\to V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W).

Then we have the following generalization of Proposition
6.11
, which follows from the same proof.

Proposition 6.23
.

Given a sufficiently large integer
d\geq 1
and any integer
d^{\prime}\geq d
, the morphisms of Equation (
6.61
) fit into a
\Gamma
-equivariant Cartesian commutative diagram

(6.62)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d^{\prime}}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d^{\prime}}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}

where the right vertical arrow is the natural inclusion. ∎

Now consider a pair of groups
\Gamma_{2}\leq\Gamma_{1}
with a pair of finite-dimensional complex
\Gamma_{1}
-representations
V
and
W
such that
V
is faithful. For a subgroup
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma_{2}
, write
\gamma_{1}^{\prime}
and
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}
the set of subgroups of
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
which are conjugate to
\Gamma^{\prime}
respectively. Using the same notation as before, write

(6.63)

\overline{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}_{2}}\subset\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W}),

the analogue of (
6.28
), which is the Zariski closure of
Z_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}_{2}}
, consisting of points whose stabilizer is conjugate to
\Gamma^{\prime}
. Then its resolution under
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
is denoted by
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}_{2}}
and it comes with a
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant morphism to
\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})
. Abusing the notations, we have the following analogue of Proposition
6.15
, which follows from the same proof.

Proposition 6.24
.

For
d
sufficiently large, after restricting to
V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}
, using the resolution functor
F^{\circ\infty}_{an}
, we have a
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant Cartesian commutative diagram

(6.64)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)^{\circ}_{\gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}_{\gamma_{2}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\check{V}_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W}),}}

where the right vertical arrow is Equation (
6.27
). ∎

Under Equation (
6.38
), the analogue of Proposition
6.18
is the following:

Proposition 6.25
.

If
V^{\prime}
is a
\Gamma
-representation, we have a
\Gamma
-equivariant Cartesian commutative diagram

(6.65)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V\oplus V^{\prime})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V\oplus V^{\prime},W\oplus V^{\prime})}.}

∎

Finally, the following is the generalization of Proposition
6.19
, for which we use the
\gamma
-version of Equation (
6.49
). Note that the conjugacy classes of subgroups of the product group
\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}
are given by pairs of conjugacy classes of
\Gamma_{1}
and
\Gamma_{2}
.

Proposition 6.26
.

The morphism (
6.49
) can be lifted to a
\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant Cartesian commutative diagram

(6.66)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})^{\circ}_{\gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times V_{2}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{1},W_{1})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})^{\circ}_{\gamma_{1}^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(V_{1}\oplus V_{2})\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1}),}}

where for
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}}(V_{1}\oplus V_{2},W_{1})^{\circ}_{\gamma_{1}^{\prime}}
, we only prescribe the conjugacy class along the
\Gamma_{1}
-factor. ∎

Similar to Section
6.3
, we can construct bundles

(6.67)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(\mathbf{V},\mathbf{W})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}

whose fibres are given by this construction. We will use the discussions in this subsection in the study of structures of equivariant complex bordisms.

7.
Regular FOP sections and resolution of singularities

In this section, we demonstrate how to construct a smooth normally complex orbifold from the zero locus of an FOP section satisfying certain regularity conditions using the machinery developed in Section
6
. Then we present our construction of manifolds from normally complex derived orbifolds, leaving the proof of invariance to the next section after introducing various versions of orbifold bordisms.

7.1.
Regular FOP sections

We describe a form of equivariant transversality of FOP sections. To distinguish it from the notion of
strong transversality
of FOP sections introduced in
[
BX22b
, Definition 6.1]
, we refer to our version as
regular
FOP sections.

Hypothesis 7.1
.

Unless otherwise stated,
M
is a smooth manifold with a
faithful

\Gamma
-action.

Remark 7.2
.

The attentive reader may worry that this somewhat restrictive assumption might preclude applications to
\Gamma
-manifolds with nontrivial generic isotropy groups, which inevitably show up in the definition of
\Gamma
-equivariant bordism. However, this does not affect the construction in this paper. Indeed, as in
[
BX22b
]
, we rely on the result of Pardon
[
Par22
]
showing that after a stabilization, any derived orbifold can be assumed to have an effective ambient space (cf. Section
8.1
). Using the geometric model of homotopical equivariant bordisms (cf. Section
8.3
), the same trick can be applied so that when discussing perturbations, so that any class in homotopical bordism can be represented by a derived manifold whose ambient space admits a faithful action. The faithfulness assumption is closely related to Lemma
6.8
and Lemma
6.22
which guarantee the generic smoothness of (strata of) the universal zero loci, following
[
BX22b
]
. It is possible to develop a more general framework directly incorporating non-faithful actions, but one would have to modify these statements and the relevant discussion.

For the next definition, we assume, as in Definition
2.23
, that
M
is equipped with with a normal complex structure and a straightened connection, and
E\to M
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant vector bundle with a normal complex structure and straightened connection. We choose in addition an integer
d
which is sufficiently large so that the construction of Lemma
6.21
results in a bundle

(7.1)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})^{\circ}

over
M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
with fibre
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(-,-)^{\circ}
. More generally, applying the construction in Equation (
6.67
), for any conjugacy class
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
of subgroups of
\Gamma^{\prime}
, we can construct the bundle

(7.2)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}

over
M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
coming the part of of the universal zero locus whose isotropy group under the
\Gamma^{\prime}
-action lies in the class
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
.

Definition 7.3
.

A smooth
\Gamma
-equivariant section
S:M\to E
is said to be
regular
at
p\in M
if the following holds. Suppose the isotropy group of
p
under the
\Gamma
-action is
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma
. Writing

(7.3)

S=(\mathring{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}):D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\to\mathring{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\oplus\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}},

we then require that the following condition hold near
p
:

(1)

\check{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
admits a normally complex lift (cf. Definition
2.23
) of degree at most
d

(7.4)

\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}:D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\to\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}).

(2)

The resulting
\Gamma^{\prime}
-equivariant bundle map

(7.5)

\displaystyle D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\to\mathring{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\times_{M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}))

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathring{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(y),(y,\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(y)))

is transverse to the smooth map

(7.6)

\{0\}\times\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}\to\mathring{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\times_{M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}))

for all conjugacy classes
\gamma^{\prime\prime}
. If such a condition holds for all points
p\in M
, we say that
S
is
regular
.

Note that the regularity condition particularly implies that
S
is transverse to
0
over the isotropy free locus of
M
. It may not be a priori clear whether the regularity condition for different subgroups are compatible, and in particular whether, given a pair of subgroups
\Gamma_{2}<\Gamma_{1}\leq\Gamma
, a choice of such regular section near
M^{\Gamma_{1}}
yields a section which satifies the regularity condition for
\Gamma_{2}
on a neighbourhood of
M^{\Gamma_{1}}
. This is the content of Proposition
7.4
below, which requires some preliminary discussion.

Let
E\to M
be as above. Using the restriction of the
\Gamma_{1}
action on the normal bundle
NM^{\Gamma_{1}}
to
\Gamma_{2}
, we obtain a decomposition

(7.7)

NM^{\Gamma_{1}}=\mathring{({NM^{\Gamma_{1}}})}^{\Gamma_{2}}\oplus\check{({NM^{\Gamma_{1}}})}^{\Gamma_{2}}

where the first component denotes the
\Gamma_{2}
-trivial isotypic piece. Using the exponential map to identify the disc bundle
D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}})
with an open neighborhood of
M^{\Gamma_{1}}
, we see that
M^{\Gamma_{2}}
can be identified with the total space of
\mathring{({NM^{\Gamma_{1}}})}^{\Gamma_{2}}
locally. As for the vector bundle
E|_{D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}})}
, we have the decomposition

(7.8)

\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}=(\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}\cap\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}})\oplus\check{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}.

Let us look at a section
S=(\mathring{S}_{\Gamma_{1}},\check{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}):D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}})\to\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\oplus\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}
with a lift
\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{1}}
of degree at most
d
. Locally near
M^{\Gamma_{1}}
, for
v=(\mathring{v},\check{v})\in NM^{\Gamma_{1}}
with respect to the decomposition in Equation (
7.7
) where
NM^{\Gamma_{1}}
is viewed as an open subset of
M
, if we write

(7.9)

\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{1}}=(\mathring{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{1}},\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{2}})

using the decomposition of Equation (
7.8
), we obtain a bundle map

(7.10)

\displaystyle D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}})

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{2}})

\displaystyle(\mathring{v},\check{v})

\displaystyle\mapsto\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{2}}(\mathring{v},\check{v})(\mathring{v},-)

covering the identity map
\mathring{v}\mapsto\mathring{v}
on
M^{\Gamma_{2}}
which is locally identified with
\mathring{({NM^{\Gamma_{1}}})}^{\Gamma_{2}}
. A word on the notations:
\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{2}}(\mathring{v},\check{v})(-,-)
stands for the polynomial determined by
\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{2}}
after evaluating at
(\mathring{v},\check{v})
in which
\check{v}
is the bundle coordinate, and the symbol
(\mathring{v},-)
is the partial evaluation map so that we can get a polynomial map with the correct domain. This is just a global version of the construction in Equation (
6.27
), with the exception that now we have a family of polynomials parametrized by
D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}})
. As a result, if we write
S=(\mathring{S}_{\Gamma_{2}},\check{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}):D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}})\to\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}\oplus\check{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}
, we have the expressions

(7.11)

\displaystyle\mathring{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}(\mathring{v},\check{v})

\displaystyle=(\mathring{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\mathring{v},\check{v}),\mathring{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{1}}(\mathring{v},\check{v})(\mathring{v},\check{v}))\in\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}=\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\oplus(\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}\cap\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}),

\displaystyle\check{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}

\displaystyle=\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{2}}(\mathring{v},\check{v})(\mathring{v},\check{v})\in\check{E}^{\Gamma_{2}},

so we know that Equation (
7.10
) defines a normally complex lift of
\check{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}
.

Proposition 7.4
.

Using the notations introduced above, if
S
is regular near
p\in M^{\Gamma_{1}}
, then near
p
, the
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant smooth map

(7.12)

\displaystyle D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}})

\displaystyle\to\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma_{2}}\times_{M^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}))

\displaystyle(\mathring{v},\check{v})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathring{S}_{\Gamma_{2}}(\mathring{v},\check{v}),((\mathring{v},\check{v}),\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{2}}(\mathring{v},\check{v})(\mathring{v},-)))

is transverse to the smooth map

(7.13)

\{0\}\times\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{2}})^{\circ}_{\gamma_{2}^{\prime}}\to\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma_{2}}\times_{M^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}),

for any conjugacy class
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}
of subgroups of
\Gamma_{2}
.

Before we proceed to the proof, we need a digression concerning transversality. The following elementary lemma is useful for showing the consistency of the regularity condition.

Lemma 7.5
.

Suppose the commutative diagram

(7.14)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{1}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces N_{2}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces M_{2}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces h}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces g}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces f_{2}}

is a pullback square of smooth manifolds such that
g
and
f_{2}
are transverse to each other. Suppose
F:Q\to M_{1}
is a smooth map which is transverse to
f_{1}
. Then the composition
g\circ F:Q\to M
is transverse to
f_{2}
.

Proof.

Take
n_{2}\in N_{2}
and
q\in Q
such that
f_{2}(n_{2})=(g\circ Q)(m_{2})
, we need to show that

(7.15)

(f_{2})_{*}(T_{n_{2}}N_{2})+g_{*}F_{*}(T_{q}Q)=T_{m_{2}}M_{2}.

Because (
7.14
) is a pullback square, there exists unique
n_{1}\in N_{1}
such that
f_{1}(n_{1})=F(q)
and
h(n_{1})=n_{2}
becasue
F(q)
and
n_{2}
are respectively mapped by
g
and
f_{2}
to the same point
m_{2}
. Using the transversality of
f_{1}
and
F
, we know that

(7.16)

F_{*}(T_{q}Q)+(f_{1})_{*}(T_{n_{1}}N_{1})=T_{F(q)}M_{1}.

Using the transversality of
g
and
f_{2}
, we have

(7.17)

g_{*}(T_{F(q)}M_{1})+(f_{2})_{*}(T_{n_{2}}N_{2})=T_{m_{2}}M_{2}.

Applying the pushforward
g_{*}
to Equation (
7.16
), we get

(7.18)

g_{*}F_{*}(T_{q}Q)+g_{*}(f_{1})_{*}(T_{n_{1}}N_{1})=g_{*}(T_{F(q)}M_{1}).

Note that
g_{*}(f_{1})_{*}=(f_{2})_{*}h_{*}
, so the above equation implies

(7.19)

g_{*}F_{*}(T_{q}Q)+(f_{2})_{*}h_{*}(T_{n_{1}}N_{1})=g_{*}(T_{F(q)}M_{1}).

Combining (
7.17
) and (
7.19
), we obtain the desired result.
∎

Proof of Proposition
7.4
.

To simply the notations in the proof, we first work out the details in the case when
M^{\Gamma_{1}}
is a single point
p
and
\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}
is trivial, and the general case can be proven using exactly the same ideas. Also, we omit the subscript by
\gamma_{2}^{\prime}
in the bundle
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{2}})^{\circ}_{\gamma_{2}^{\prime}}
.

Recall the notations from Section
6.2
, and write
NM^{\Gamma_{1}}
as a finite-dimensional complex and faithful
\Gamma_{1}
-representation
V
and
\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}
a finite-dimensional complex
\Gamma_{1}
-representation
W
. We identify
M^{\Gamma_{2}}
with
\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}
, over which we have the
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant vector bundles
\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}
and
\underline{W}=\underline{\mathring{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\oplus\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}
. The data of a normally complex lift of a smooth
\Gamma
-equivariant section near
M^{\Gamma_{1}}
is a smooth
\Gamma_{1}
-equivariant map

(7.20)

f:V\to\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)

near
0\in V
with associated section

(7.21)

\displaystyle S_{f}:V

\displaystyle\to W

\displaystyle v

\displaystyle\mapsto f(v)(v).

Under such a lift,
S_{f}
is regular if and only if the graph map of
f
is transverse to the map
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)^{\circ}\to V\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)
. Now for a vector
v^{\prime}\in\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}
with isotropy group
\Gamma_{2}
near
0
, write the induced normal complex lift of
S_{f}
near
v^{\prime}
as

(7.22)

\displaystyle(\mathring{S}_{f},\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{f}):\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}

\displaystyle\to\underline{W}=\underline{\mathring{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\oplus(\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}_{1}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}))

\displaystyle(\mathring{v},\check{v})

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathring{f}(\mathring{v},\check{v})(\mathring{v},\check{v}),\check{f}(\mathring{v},\check{v})(\mathring{v},-)),

where we have written
f=(\mathring{f},\check{f})
under the decomposition
W=\mathring{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}\oplus\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}
. We need to verify that the above map is transverse to
\{0\}\times\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}})^{\circ}\to\underline{W}=\underline{\mathring{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\oplus(\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}_{1}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}))
.

Note that we have a
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant morphism

(7.23)

\displaystyle\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})

\displaystyle\to\underline{\mathring{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\oplus\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}})

\displaystyle((\mathring{v},\check{v}),\mathring{P}_{\mathring{v}},\check{P}_{\mathring{v}})

\displaystyle\mapsto((\mathring{v},\mathring{P}_{\mathring{v}}(\mathring{v})),\check{P}_{\mathring{v}})

where we use the decomposition
\underline{W}=\underline{\mathring{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\oplus\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}
and
\mathring{P}_{\mathring{v}},\check{P}_{\mathring{v}}
are the fiberwise polynomials over
\mathring{v}
. This is a smooth morphism of algebraic varieties, thus by using the functorial resolution Theorem
6.2
, we obtain a
\Gamma_{2}
-equivariant Cartesian diagram

(7.24)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}_{1}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{W})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\times\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathring{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\oplus\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}})}}

of compatible resolutions of the universal zero loci. By concatenating the above diagram with Diagram
6.31
, we obtain a Cartesian diagram of smooth varieties

(7.25)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}},W)^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{V_{+}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(V,W)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\times\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\underline{\mathring{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}}\oplus\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}\times_{\mathring{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}_{+}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(\underline{\check{V}^{\Gamma_{2}}},\underline{\check{W}^{\Gamma_{2}}})}.}

Now we can apply Lemma
7.5
to the graph map of
f
, which establishes the desired transversality result for
(\mathring{S}_{f},\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{f})
for near
v^{\prime}
because
v^{\prime}
lies in the open subset
V^{\Gamma_{2}}_{+}
.

In general, using the bundles constructed in Section
6.3
, we can assemble the fiberwise diagram (
7.25
), we get

(7.26)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\times\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\oplus NM^{\Gamma_{1}}\times_{M^{\Gamma_{1}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\times\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{2}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma_{2}}\times_{M^{\Gamma_{2}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{2}}(NM^{\Gamma_{2}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}))}.}

Then the same argument shows that regularity holds near points with isotropy group
\Gamma_{2}
.
∎

Proposition
7.4
can be summarized as the
openness
of FOP regularity. This ensures the compatibility of resolutions of singularities which will be used later.

Because the regularity is a notion of equivariant transversality, it might be possible that it can never be achieved. However, in our situation, it exactly meets the sufficient condition to achieve equivariant transversality as observed by Wasserman
[
Was69
]
and discussed in detail in
[
Par21
, Section 5.4]
.

Lemma 7.6
.

Assume that we are in the situation of Definition
7.3
. Furthermore, assume that
M
is compact (or has compact closure as a subspace in a topological space). Then a generic choice of FOP section is regular.

Proof.

Note that there is no difficulty to achieve the transversality of
\mathring{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
, because this is a non-equivariant situation. Therefore, after choosing
\mathring{S}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
generically, we are reduced to the toy case when
\mathring{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}=\{0\}
. In other words, we want to show for the graph of a generic
\Gamma^{\prime}
-equivariant smooth map

(7.27)

\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}:D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\to\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})

is transverse to

(7.28)

\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}\to NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\times_{M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}).

Because we are taking the graph of
\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
, for any
y\in D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}})
, the pushforward of the tangent space of
D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}})
at
y
by the differential
d(\mathrm{graph}(\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}))
must be surjective onto
T(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\subset T(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\times_{M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}))
. Therefore, in order to guarantee that
\mathrm{graph}(\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}})
meets transversely with
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
at
(v,P)=(\mathring{v},\check{v},P)
, we just need to make sure that
d(\mathrm{graph}(\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}))
is surjective onto the complement of the image of
T\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}
in
T_{P}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(N_{\mathring{v}}M^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}_{\mathring{v}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})
. Note that the space in which
\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
lives is the Fréchet space of smooth
\Gamma^{\prime}
-equivariant bundle maps

(7.29)

C^{\infty}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}),\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})).

In the special case when
M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
is a point, if the fibers of
NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
and
\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
correspond to complex
\Gamma^{\prime}
-representations
V^{\prime}
and
W^{\prime}
respectively, the above space is

(7.30)

C^{\infty}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}(V^{\prime},\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(V^{\prime},W^{\prime})).

Because constant maps from
V^{\prime}
to
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(V^{\prime},W^{\prime})
are
\Gamma^{\prime}
-equivariant, an application of the Sard–Smale theorem (one can either use Taubes’ trick or Floer’s
C_{\epsilon}^{\infty}
space to set up the Banach spaces) shows that a generic
\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
satisfies the desired transversality condition. In general, using the compactness assumption of
M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
, a covering argument then implies the result.
∎

Note that the notion of regularity
a priori
depends on the choice of cut-off degree
d
. The next statement shows that such a dependence can be removed.

Lemma 7.7
.

We working is the same setting as in Definition
7.3
: for any pair of integers
d\leq d^{\prime}
, if
S
is a regular FOP section of degree at most
d
, it is also regular by viewing the lift as a normally complex lift of degree at most
d^{\prime}
.

Proof.

By definition, the map in Equation (
7.5
) is transverse to the map in Equation (
7.6
). Using Corollary
6.11
and the construction in Lemma
6.21
, we have a Cartesian diagram

(7.31)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\oplus\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}}}
