{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\times_{M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}))}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\oplus\tilde{Z}_{d^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\times_{M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})).}}

Just as in the case of Proposition
7.4
, the fact that the Cartesian nature the above diagram and Lemma
7.5
implies that (
7.5
) is transverse to the
d^{\prime}
-version of (
7.6
).
∎

After the discussions in the equivariant setting, we can go back to orbifolds.

Definition 7.8
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
be a vector bundle over an
effective
orbifold, which are both endowed with normal complex structures and straightenings as in Definition
2.24
. Then an FOP section
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
with a normally complex lift of degree at most
d
is called
regular
if its pullback as a section of
E_{\alpha}\to U_{\alpha}
is regular in the sense of Definition
7.3
.

Note that to make sure that varieties of the form
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma}(V,W)^{\circ}
have the correct dimension, we need to make
d
sufficiently large. In our applications, such a uniform choice can be made after introducing certain compactness assumptions.

Proposition 7.9
.

Let
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
be a vector bundle over an
effective
orbifold, endowed with normal complex structures and straightenings as in Definition
2.24
. Suppose that
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
is a smooth section such that the zero locus
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
is contained in an open suborbifold
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{U}}
with the property that
|{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}|
is precompact. Then there exists an integer
d_{0}\geq 1
such that for any
d\geq d_{0}
, and any
\epsilon>0
, there exists a smooth section
{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
satisfying the following properties:

(1)

in an open neighborhood of
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
whose image in
|{\mathcal{U}}|
contains
|\overline{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}|
, the section
{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon}
is a regular FOP section of degree at most
d
;

(2)

\|{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon}-{\mathcal{S}}\|_{C^{0}}<\epsilon
;

(3)

for
\epsilon
sufficiently small, the set
|{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)|
is compact.

Proof.

The proof is exactly the same as the proof of
[
BX22b
, Proposition 6.4]
, with the following changes: (i) the existence over an étale chart as addressed by
[
BX22b
, Lemma 6.5]
is replaced by Lemma
7.6
, and (ii) to apply the two-layer induction proof of
[
BX22b
, Proposition 6.4]
, the openness of strong transversality is replaced by the openness of regularity as proven in Proposition
7.4
.
∎

For the next topic of this subsection, we discuss the behavior of such sections under stabilization. Namely, assume that we are in the situation of Definition
7.8
. Let
\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}:{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{U}}
be another normally complex vector bundle and write
\tau_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}:{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\to\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
for the tautological section. Assume that
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
is endowed with a straightening, so we have a notion of FOP sections for
\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}\oplus\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
.

Lemma 7.10
.

If
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
is regular, then so is
\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}\oplus\tau_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}:{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\to\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}\oplus\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
.

Proof.

We just need to check the statement over étale charts, so let us assume that we are in the situation of Definition
7.3
and
\pi_{E^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to M
is a
\Gamma
-equivariant vector bundle with a normal complex structure, and we have the tautological section
\tau_{E^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}E^{\prime}
. Observe that for any
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma
, we have an inclusion
(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\subset\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{-1}(M^{\Gamma^{\prime}})
. As a result, the
\Gamma
-equivariant section
\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}S\oplus\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\tau_{E^{\prime}}
admits a normally complex lift of degree at most
d
.

For
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma
, the normal bundle
N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
admits a projection map to the normal bundle
NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
. It induces a splitting

(7.32)

N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}=N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\parallel}\oplus N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\perp},

where
N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\perp}
is the kernel of the projection map and
N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\parallel}
is the orthogonal complement under the induced metric. This is indeed a splitting of vector bundles when restricted to connected components. On the other hand, over
N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
, we have splittings

(7.33)

\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}E=\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\mathring{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\oplus\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\textrm{and }\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}E=\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\mathring{E^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\oplus\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{E^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}.

Then the section

(7.34)

\displaystyle S^{\tau}:=\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}S\oplus\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\tau_{E^{\prime}}

\displaystyle=(\mathring{S}^{\tau}_{\Gamma^{\prime}},\check{S}^{\tau}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}):

\displaystyle D_{r}(N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\to(\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\mathring{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\oplus\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\mathring{E^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\oplus(\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}\oplus\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{E^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})

has normally complex lift

(7.35)

\displaystyle\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\tau}:D_{r/2}(N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\subset D_{r}(N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\parallel})\oplus D_{r}(N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\perp})

\displaystyle\to\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\parallel},\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\oplus\mathrm{Poly}_{1}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\perp},\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{E^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})

\displaystyle\subset\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\oplus\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}(N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}},\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{E^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}})

written as the diagonal matrix
\mathrm{diag}(\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}},\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\tau_{E^{\prime}})
, in which we have identified
N(E^{\prime})^{\Gamma^{\prime}}_{\perp}
with
\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\check{E^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
. Then we see that
\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}^{\tau}
is obtained from
\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma^{\prime}}
via a fiberwise/family version of the stabilization map in Equation (
6.38
). Then the regularity of
S^{\tau}
simply follows from the Cartesian property of Diagram (
6.65
) and the fact that the tautogical section is transverse to
0
along the
\Gamma^{\prime}
-trivial part
\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\mathring{E^{\prime}}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
.
∎

The final statements of this subsection are the following.

Lemma 7.11
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}:{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
be an FOP section which is regular. Suppose
{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
is another effective normally complex orbifold. Then under the projection map

\pi_{1}:{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{U}_{2}}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{1},

the pulled-back section
\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}_{1}
is also FOP regular.

Proof.

Again, we can check the assertion in the equivariant setting for
\Gamma_{1}
-equivariant triples
(M_{1},E_{1},S_{1})
and the
\Gamma_{2}
-manifold
M_{2}
. Then the relevant Cartesian commutative diagram is

(7.36)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\oplus(NM_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}\times\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}(NM_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}})^{\circ})_{\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\begin{aligned} \pi_{2}^{*}\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}|_{M_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times M_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}}\oplus((NM_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times NM_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}})\times_{M_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times M_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}}\\
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}(NM_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}},\pi_{2}^{*}\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}|_{M_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times M_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}}))\end{aligned}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\oplus\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\times\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}(NM_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times NM_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}},\pi_{2}^{*}\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}|_{M_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times M_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}})^{\circ}_{\gamma_{1}^{\prime\prime}\times\{1\}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\begin{aligned} \pi_{2}^{*}\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}|_{M_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times M_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}}\oplus((NM_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times NM_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}})\times_{M_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times M_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}}\\
\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\times\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}(NM_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times NM_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}},\pi_{2}^{*}\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}|_{M_{1}^{\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}}\times M_{2}^{\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}}}))\end{aligned}}}

over the
\Gamma_{1}^{\prime}\times\Gamma_{2}^{\prime}\leq\Gamma_{1}\times\Gamma_{2}
-invariant locus of
M_{1}\times M_{2}
under the product action, which is the fiberwise version of the diagram (
6.50
). The right vertical map is constructed using the bundle vesion of (
6.49
). Then as in the case of Proposition
7.4
, the Cartesian nature the above diagram and Lemma
7.5
imply the desired claim.
∎

Corollary 7.12
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}:{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
be an FOP section which is regular. Suppose
{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
is a (not necessarily effective) normally complex orbifold. Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\xrightarrow{\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
be a complex vector bundle such that the total space
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
is an effective orbifold. Then the section

\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\times\tau_{{\mathcal{F}}_{2}}:{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\to\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{E}}_{1}\oplus\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{2}}^{*}{\mathcal{F}}_{2},

where
\pi_{1}:{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
denotes the projection, is FOP regular.

Proof.

This follows from the Cartesian diagram in Lemma
7.11
and Lemma
7.10
because the above section is a stabilization of the section
\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}
(here we abuse the notation to use
\pi_{1}
to denote the projection
{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\times{\mathcal{U}_{2}}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{1}
) by the bundle
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
.
∎

Remark 7.13
.

Following Remark
7.2
, we include the above corollary since we formulated FOP regularity only for effective orbifolds.

7.2.
Resolution of zero loci: isotropy-free part

Now we describe how to construct a resolution of the closure of the isotropy-free part of the zero locus of a regular FOP section defined over an effective normally complex orbifold via pulling back the resolution produced from Theorem
6.2
. We explain the construction in the equivariant setting first, which we then globalize to the orbifold setting.

Let
E\to M
be as in Definition
7.3
and suppose that
S:M\to E
is a regular
\Gamma
-equivariant smooth section which admits a normally complex lift of degree at most
d
. By definition,
S
comes with lifts defined over the disc bundles over the fixed point loci. If we range over all proper subgroups
\Gamma^{\prime}<\Gamma
, we see that the collection of open subsets

(7.37)

\{U(M,\Gamma^{\prime},r):=D_{r}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}})\setminus\big(\bigcup_{\ \Gamma^{\prime}<\Gamma^{\prime\prime}}D_{\leq r/2}(NM^{\Gamma^{\prime\prime}})\big)\}_{\{1\}\neq\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma}

together with the open subset
forms an open cover of
M
. Then we can construct a new open cover as follows: observe that the set of subgroups of
\Gamma
forms a poset under inclusion. We can form a new poset
2^{\Gamma}
from it, whose elements are given by a chain of subgroups

(7.38)

\Gamma_{\vec{k}}:=(\Gamma_{1}<\cdots<\Gamma_{k}),\text{ where }\Gamma_{i}\leq\Gamma\text{ for }1\leq i\leq k,

and the partial order is induced by the inverse inclusion of chains. Define

(7.39)

U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r):=\bigcap_{1\leq i\leq k}U(M,\Gamma_{i},r),

we see that
U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)\subset U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}^{\prime}},r)
if and only if
\Gamma_{\vec{k}}\leq\Gamma_{\vec{k}^{\prime}}
. Moreover, if
\Gamma_{k}
and
\Gamma_{k^{\prime}}
are respectively the maximal elements in
\Gamma_{\vec{k}}
and
\Gamma_{\vec{k}^{\prime}}
, the inclusion
U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)\subset U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}^{\prime}},r)
is equivariant under the group embedding
\Gamma_{k^{\prime}}\to\Gamma_{k}
. Therefore,

(7.40)

\{U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)\}_{\Gamma_{\vec{k}}\in 2^{\Gamma}}

defines an open cover of
M
and for any two members
U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)
and we can present
M
as

(7.41)

\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{\Gamma_{\vec{k}}\in 2^{\Gamma}}U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)

in which the arrows are inclusion of open subsets, which are compatible with the partial order on
2^{\Gamma}
. For
\Gamma_{\vec{k}}=(\Gamma_{1}<\cdots<\Gamma_{k})
, because any
U(M,\Gamma^{\prime},r)
is invariant under the
\Gamma^{\prime}
-action, we see that
U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)
is invariant under the
\Gamma_{1}
-action. By restricting the
\Gamma_{1}
-version of the equivariant map (
7.5
) to
U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)\subset U(M,\Gamma_{1},r)
, we obtain a smooth map

(7.42)

\displaystyle U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)

\displaystyle\to\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma_{1}}\times_{M^{\Gamma_{1}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}))

\displaystyle y

\displaystyle\mapsto(\mathring{S}_{\Gamma_{1}}(y),(y,\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{1}}(y))),

we define
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)}
to be the fiber product

(7.43)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\oplus\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}})^{\circ}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma_{1}}\times_{M^{\Gamma_{1}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}))}.}
(
7.6
)
(
7.42
)

Because
S
has been assumed to be regular,
\tilde{U}(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)
is a smooth
\Gamma_{1}
-manifold with an induced normal complex structure from the ones on
M
and
E
.

Lemma 7.14
.

The equivariant inclusion map
U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)\subset U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}^{\prime}},r)
for
\Gamma_{\vec{k}}\leq\Gamma_{\vec{k}^{\prime}}
induces an equivariant open embedding
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)}\to\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}^{\prime}},r)}
which respects the normal complex structures.

Proof.

This is a straightforward consequence of the Cartesian nature of the diagram (
7.26
) after decomposing the local lifts of
S
in the form of (
7.12
).
∎

Using the colimit presentation, we see that

(7.44)

S^{-1}(0)=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{\Gamma_{\vec{k}}\in 2^{\Gamma}}\big(S^{-1}(0)\cap U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)\big).

Based on Lemma
7.14
, we can define a resolution of the closure of the isotropy-free locus of
S^{-1}(0)
to be

(7.45)

\tilde{S}^{-1}(0):=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{\Gamma_{\vec{k}}\in 2^{\Gamma}}\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)}.

Proposition 7.15
.

The smooth manifold
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)
is a
\Gamma
-manifold with a normal complex structure which admits a
\Gamma
-equivariant map to
S^{-1}(0)
. This map defines a diffeomorphism over the isotropy-free part of
S^{-1}(0)
. Furthermore,
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)
is independent of the choice of sufficiently small
r>0
.

Finally, for any open embedding
M^{\prime}\to M
which is equivariant under an injective group homomorphism
\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
such that
E^{\prime}=E|_{M^{\prime}}
and
S^{\prime}=S|_{M^{\prime}}
with compatible straightenings and normal complex structures, there is an equivariant open embedding
(\tilde{S}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)\to\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)
covering
(S^{\prime})^{-1}(0)\to S^{-1}(0)
which respects the normal complex structures.

Proof.

Note that for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
, if we define

(7.46)

\gamma\Gamma_{\vec{k}}\gamma^{-1}:=(\gamma\Gamma_{1}\gamma^{-1}<\cdots<\gamma\Gamma_{k}\gamma^{-1}),

we see that
U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)
is mapped isomorphically onto
U(M,\gamma\Gamma_{\vec{k}}\gamma^{-1},r)
by the
\gamma
-translation. Its compatibility with the arrows in (
7.41
) recovers the
\Gamma
-action on
M
. Such an isomorphism can also be lifted to an isomorphism between the vector bundles over them, and it intertwines with the normal complex structures and straightenings. As a result, the corresponding (fiberwise) universal zero loci are identified, and so are their resolutions. Then we see that the colimit presentation of
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)
inherits a
\Gamma
-action, which defines a smooth
\Gamma
-action on
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)
. The existence of normal complex structure can be checked locally. Indeed, in the diagram (
7.43
), the vertical arrow (
7.6
) comes from a complex algebraic map, therefore respects the complex, in particular, normal complex structure. For the horizontal arrow (
7.42
), it suffices to check that its linearization respects the complex structures along the
\Gamma_{1}
-fixed point locus. This follows from the fact that the fibers of
NM^{\Gamma_{1}}
does not contain any
\Gamma_{1}
-trivial summand because this implies that the differential of
\check{\mathfrak{s}}_{\Gamma_{1}}
is necessarily trivial along the zero section
M^{\Gamma_{1}}
: we are only left with the identity map along the fibers of
NM^{\Gamma_{1}}
, which is evidently complex linear. By definition,
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)
maps diffeomorphically onto
S^{-1}(0)
over the isotropy free locus, over which the resolution does nothing. The assertion that
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)
is independent of
r
follows by inspection. The functoriality of the resolution under open embeddings is a straightforward consequence of the construction.
∎

With these preparations in the equivariant setting, we can discuss the situation for orbifolds. Let
{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}}
be as in Definition
7.8
. Denote by
S_{\alpha}:U_{\alpha}\to E_{\alpha}
be the pullback of
{\mathcal{S}}
to the étale chart
U_{\alpha}\to{\mathcal{U}}
. Writing
U=\coprod_{\alpha}U_{\alpha}
, from the discussions in Section
2
, we can present
{\mathcal{U}}
using the topological groupoid
[U\times_{\mathcal{U}}U\rightrightarrows U]
. Similarly, if we write
E=\coprod_{\alpha}E_{\alpha}
, there is the associated groupoid
[E\times_{\mathcal{E}}E\rightrightarrows E]
of
{\mathcal{E}}
. Then we can consider

(7.47)

\coprod_{\alpha}\tilde{S}_{\alpha}^{-1}(0)

the disjoint union of the equivariant resolutions of
S_{\alpha}^{-1}(0)
. By the definition of straightenings and normal complex structures, the functoriality part of Proposition
7.15
implies that the topological groupoid
[U\times_{\mathcal{U}}U\rightrightarrows U]
restricts to
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)\subset U
and it produces a normally complex orbifold, which we denote by
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
. The following is the counterpart of Proposition
7.15
in the orbifold setting. We state it for completeness and its proof should be evident.

Proposition 7.16
.

The smooth normally complex orbifold
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
depends only on the straightenings, normal complex structures on
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}
, and the FOP section
{\mathcal{S}}
. It admits a map onto
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
, which is an isomorphism of orbifolds over the isotropy-free part of
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
.

For any open embedding
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{U}}
such that
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{E}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{S}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
with compatible straightenings and normal complex structures, there is an equivariant open embedding
(\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)\to\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
covering
({\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)\to{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
which respects the normal complex structures. ∎

Next, we need a statement concerning the resolution of the zero locus of a stabilization.

Proposition 7.17
.

Suppose that we are in the situation of Lemma
7.10
. Then the resolution of the isotropy-free part of
({\mathcal{S}}^{\tau})^{-1}(0):=(\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}\oplus\tau_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}})^{-1}(0)
agrees with
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
.

Proof.

Lemma
7.10
proves that
{\mathcal{S}}^{\tau}
is regular. The zero loci
({\mathcal{S}}^{\tau})^{-1}(0)
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
agree with each other. Then the compatibility of resolutions as exhibited in Proposition
6.18
implies that the resolutions also coincide, by following the information provided by the lift (
7.35
).
∎

Finally, we formulate a proposition about products.

Proposition 7.18
.

Suppose that we are in the situation of Lemma
7.11
. Then the resolution
\tilde{(\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{S}})}^{-1}(0)
agrees with
\tilde{{\mathcal{S}}}^{-1}(0)\times{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
if
{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
is effective.

Proof.

By Lemma
7.11
, we see that the pullback
\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}
is regular. The Cartesian nature of the diagram in the proof implies the compatibility of resolutions. When
{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
is effective, the isotropy-free part of
(\pi_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{S}})^{-1}(0)
agrees with the isotropy-free part of
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
with the isotropy-free locus of
{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
so we get the whole
{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
-factor.
∎

7.3.
Resolution with prescribed isotropy

Following the discussion in Section
6.4
, we explain how to extend the construction in the previous subsection to obtain a resolution of the (closure of) the zero locus of a regular FOP section with a prescribed isomorphism class of stabilizer groups. We will not use the results in this subsection in the final discussion, but we include it here to get the reader prepared with the construction in the next subsection which may seem confusing for the first reading.

Following the equivariant set up in Section
7.2
, the additional choice to make here is a finite group
\tilde{\Gamma}
which is isomorphic to a subgroup of
\Gamma
. Then we can consider the open subsets
U(M,\Gamma^{\prime},r)
from Equation (
7.37
) with the requirement that
\tilde{\Gamma}
is isomorphic to a subgroup of
\Gamma^{\prime}
. Introducing a new notation to imply the dependence on
\tilde{\Gamma}
, we will denote such an open subset by

(7.48)

U(M,\Gamma^{\prime},r)_{\tilde{\Gamma}}.

The union of all such
U(M,\Gamma^{\prime},r)_{\tilde{\Gamma}}
defines an open subset of
M
which contains the fixed point locus under any subgroup of
\Gamma
which is isomorphic to
\tilde{\Gamma}
. We can similarly define

(7.49)

U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)_{\tilde{\Gamma}}:=\bigcap_{1\leq i\leq k}U(M,\Gamma_{i},r)_{\tilde{\Gamma}}

for chains of subgroups
\Gamma_{\vec{k}}=(\Gamma_{1}<\cdots<\Gamma_{k})
such that
\Gamma_{1}
admits a subgroup isomorphic to
\tilde{\Gamma}
. Using the map (
7.42
), instead of taking the fiber product as in Diagram (
7.43
), we can define
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r),\tilde{\gamma}}
to be the fiber product

(7.50)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r),\tilde{\gamma}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\{0\}\oplus\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{U(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathring{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}\oplus(NM^{\Gamma_{1}}\times_{M^{\Gamma_{1}}}\mathrm{Poly}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}}))},}
(
7.6
)
(
7.42
)

where
\gamma^{\prime}
stands for the conjugacy class of subgroups of
\Gamma_{1}
conjugate to the chosen subgroup isomorphic to
\tilde{\Gamma}
, and
\tilde{Z}_{d}^{\Gamma_{1}}(NM^{\Gamma_{1}},\check{E}^{\Gamma_{1}})^{\circ}_{\gamma^{\prime}}
is the bundle from Equation (
6.67
). Similar to Lemma
7.14
, the compatibility of the resolutions (now we view
\gamma^{\prime}
representing a conjugacy class of subgroups in this chain of finite groups) ensures that the colimit

(7.51)

\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}:=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{\Gamma_{\vec{k}}}\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\Gamma_{\vec{k}},r),\tilde{\gamma}}

is well-defined and it admits a smooth map onto the closure of the subset of
S^{-1}(0)
whose isotropy group under the
\Gamma
-action is isomorphic to
\tilde{\Gamma}
. Similar to Proposition
7.15
, we conclude the following.

Proposition 7.19
.

The smooth manifold
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}
is a
\Gamma
-manifold with a normal complex structure which admits a
\Gamma
-equivariant map to
S^{-1}(0)
. This map defines a diffeomorphism over the part of
S^{-1}(0)
whose isotropy group under the
\Gamma
-action is isomorphic to
\tilde{\Gamma}
. Furthermore,
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}
is independent of the choice of sufficiently small
r>0
.

For any open embedding
M^{\prime}\to M
which is equivariant under an injective group homomorphism
\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
such that
E^{\prime}=E|_{M^{\prime}}
and
S^{\prime}=S|_{M^{\prime}}
with compatible straightenings and normal complex structures, there is an equivariant open embedding
(\tilde{S}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}\to\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}
which respects the normal complex structures, provided that
\tilde{\Gamma}
is isomorphic to a subgroup of
\Gamma^{\prime}
. ∎

As before, we can globalize the discussion to the orbifold setting: given the finite group
\tilde{\Gamma}
, we can consider the analog of (
7.47
)

(7.52)

\coprod_{\alpha}\tilde{S}_{\alpha}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}

which can be patched together to define an orbifold, denoted by
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}
. Then the following statement holds for the same reasons as before.

Proposition 7.20
.

The smooth normally complex orbifold
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}
depends only on the straightenings, normal complex structures on
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}
, and the regular FOP section
{\mathcal{S}}
.

For any open embedding
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{U}}
such that
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{E}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{S}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
with compatible straightenings and normal complex structures, there is an equivariant open embedding
(\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}
which respects the normal complex structures.

If we are in the situation of Lemma
7.10
, then the resolution
({\mathcal{S}}^{\tau})^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}:=(\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}\oplus\tau_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}})^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}
agrees with
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}
. ∎

7.4.
Resolution of prescribed isotropy type

In the previous subsection, we discussed how to resolve the zero loci of FOP regular sections with prescribed stabilizers. We need a slight refinement, which allows us to further decompose such resolutions into pieces indexed by certain representation-theoretic data. This will be used to ensure the module map property of the splitting map to be described in the next section.

Definition 7.21
.

An
isotropy type
is a triple

(7.53)

\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}=(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{V},\tilde{W})

where
\tilde{\Gamma}
is a finite group, while
\tilde{V}
and
\tilde{W}
are finite-dimensional complex
\tilde{\Gamma}
-representations which do not contain any trivial subrepresentation (of non-trivial rank). An isomorphism of isotropy types means an isomorphism of groups which intertwine the corresponding representations.

Given a finite group
\Gamma
, we can consider the set of isotropy types where the group
\tilde{\Gamma}
is a subgroup of
\Gamma
. We say this is the set of isotropy types
subordinate to

\Gamma
. This set has three features.

(1)

It admits a conjugation action by
\Gamma
: for any
\gamma\in\Gamma
and an isotropy type
(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{V},\tilde{W})
with
\tilde{\Gamma}\leq\Gamma
, we can form a new isotropy type with group given by
\gamma\tilde{\Gamma}\gamma^{-1}
and the representations are induced by the conjugation action. Therefore, in this setting, it makes sense to talk about
conjugacy classes
of isotropy types.

(2)

This set has a natural partial order. Namely, we declare that

(7.54)

(\tilde{\Gamma}_{2},\tilde{V}_{2},\tilde{W}_{2})\leq(\tilde{\Gamma}_{1},\tilde{V}_{1},\tilde{W}_{1})

if
\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}\leq\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}
, and if we restrict the representations
\tilde{V}_{1}
and
\tilde{W}_{1}
along the subgroup
\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}
and write the decomposition we had before as

(7.55)

\tilde{V}_{1}=\mathring{\tilde{V}}_{1}^{\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}}\oplus\check{\tilde{V}}_{1}^{\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}},\quad\quad\quad\tilde{W}_{1}=\mathring{\tilde{W}}_{1}^{\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}}\oplus\check{\tilde{W}}_{1}^{\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}},

then we have
\tilde{V}_{2}\cong\check{\tilde{V}}_{1}^{\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}}
and
\tilde{W}_{2}\cong\check{\tilde{W}}_{1}^{\tilde{\Gamma}_{2}}
.

(3)

There is a
stabilization
operation. Namely, given
(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{V},\tilde{W})
, if we have another finite-dimensional complex
\tilde{\Gamma}
-representation
\tilde{V}^{\prime}
without trivial summands,

(7.56)

(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{V}\oplus\tilde{V}^{\prime},\tilde{W}\oplus\tilde{V}^{\prime})

is another well-defined isotropy type.

In the more abstract setting, where the group
\tilde{\Gamma}
does not necessarily arise from a subgroup of a given group, we can consider the collection of all isomorphism classes of isotropy types. This set inherits the last two features listed above.

(1)

We have that
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}_{2}\leq\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}_{1}
, for isomorphism classes
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}_{2}
and
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}_{1}
, if and only if the group in
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}_{2}
admits a embedding as a subgroup of the group in
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}_{1}
, and the representations pull back as described above.

(2)

We can stabilize an isomorphism class of isotropy types
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}=[(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{V},\tilde{W})]
by finite-dimensional complex
\tilde{\Gamma}
-representation
\tilde{V}^{\prime}
without trivial summands, which gives arise to a well-defined isomorphism classes.

In either case, we call a class from the set of isotropy types modulo the equivalence relation generated by stabilizations a
stable isotropy type
.

Geometrically, such consideration naturally shows up as follows. For the
\Gamma
-equivariant version, continuing the setup in Section
7.1
, we consider a
\Gamma
-equivariant
E\to M
. Given a subgroup
\Gamma^{\prime}\leq\Gamma
and any component of
M^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
, the fibers of
NM^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
and
\check{E}^{\Gamma^{\prime}}
are naturally complex
\Gamma^{\prime}
-representations without trivial summands. Therefore, we can further decompose the submanifold of
M
whose isotropy group is
\Gamma^{\prime}
into a union indexed by isotropy types whose underlying group is
\Gamma^{\prime}
. Accordingly, by shrinking
r
if necessary, we can refine the open subsets from (
7.37
) into

(7.57)

U(M,\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},r),

arising from (punctured) disc bundles over points with prescribed isotropy type
\bm{\gamma}^{\prime}
. Then given an isotropy type
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}
isomorphic to one which is subordinate to
\Gamma
, we can consider the open subsets
U(M,\bm{\gamma}^{\prime},r)
such that
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}\leq\bm{\gamma}^{\prime}
. We can similarly define

(7.58)

U(M,\bm{\gamma}_{\vec{k}},r)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}:=\bigcap_{1\leq i\leq k}U(M,\bm{\gamma}_{i},r)

for chains of isotropy types
\bm{\gamma}_{\vec{k}}=(\bm{\gamma}_{1}<\cdots<\bm{\gamma}_{k})
such that
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}\leq\bm{\gamma}_{1}
. Then we obtain the colimit

(7.59)

\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}:=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{\bm{\gamma}_{\vec{k}}}\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\bm{\gamma}_{\vec{k}},r),\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}

where
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{(M,\bm{\gamma}_{\vec{k}},r),\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}
is obtained from the pullback diagram (
7.50
) replacing the bottom left entry by the open subset
U(M,\bm{\gamma}_{\vec{k}},r)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}
.
It is well-defined and it admits a smooth map onto the closure of the subset of
S^{-1}(0)
whose isotropy type under the
\Gamma
-action is isomorphic to
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}
.

Proposition 7.22
.

The smooth manifold
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}
is a
\Gamma
-manifold with a normal complex structure which admits a
\Gamma
-equivariant map to
S^{-1}(0)
. This map defines a diffeomorphism over the part of
S^{-1}(0)
whose isotropy type under the
\Gamma
-action is isomorphic to
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}
. Furthermore,
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\gamma}}
is independent of the choice of sufficiently small
r>0
.

For any open embedding
M^{\prime}\to M
which is equivariant under an injective group homomorphism
\Gamma^{\prime}\to\Gamma
such that
E^{\prime}=E|_{M^{\prime}}
and
S^{\prime}=S|_{M^{\prime}}
with compatible straightenings and normal complex structures, there is an equivariant open embedding
(\tilde{S}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}\to\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}
which respects the normal complex structures, provided that
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}
is isomorphic to an isotropy type subordinate to
\Gamma^{\prime}
. ∎

Globalizing to the orbifold setting, the generalization of (
7.52
) is now given by

(7.60)

\coprod_{\alpha}\tilde{S}_{\alpha}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}},

and the resulting orbifold is written as
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}
, coming from all isotropy types lying in the isomorphism class
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}
.

Proposition 7.23
.

The smooth normally complex orbifold
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}
depends only on the straightenings, normal complex structures on
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}
, and the regular FOP section
{\mathcal{S}}
.

For any open embedding
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{U}}
such that
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{E}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}={\mathcal{S}}|_{{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}}
with compatible straightenings and normal complex structures, there is an equivariant open embedding
(\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}\to\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}
which respects the normal complex structures. ∎

To discuss stabilization, it is more convenient to work with stable isotropy types. Given a stable isotropy type
[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]
, the notations

(7.61)

\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}\quad\quad\quad\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}

simply denote the disjoint union of all
\tilde{S}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}
and
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}}
such that
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}
lies in the class
[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]
. The following proposition follows from the reasoning behind Proposition
7.20
.

Proposition 7.24
.

In the of situation of Lemma
7.10
, then the resolution
({\mathcal{S}}^{\tau})^{-1}(0)_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}:=(\pi_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}\oplus\tau_{{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}})^{-1}(0)_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
agrees with
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
. ∎

Finally, note that isotropy admits a product structure, sending a pair
\bm{\gamma}_{1}=(\tilde{\Gamma}_{1},\tilde{V}_{1},\tilde{W}_{1})
and
\bm{\gamma}_{2}=(\tilde{\Gamma}_{2},\tilde{V}_{2},\tilde{W}_{2})
to

(7.62)

\bm{\gamma}_{1}\times\bm{\gamma}_{2}:=(\tilde{\Gamma}_{1}\times\tilde{\Gamma}_{2},\tilde{V}_{1}\times\tilde{V}_{2},\tilde{W}_{1}\times\tilde{W}_{2}),

and it is well-defined after passing to isomorphism classes and stabilization. We have the following generalization of Proposition
7.18
.

Proposition 7.25
.

Let
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}:{\mathcal{U}}_{1}\to{\mathcal{E}}_{1}
be a regular FOP section. Suppose
{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
is a (not necessarily effective) normally complex orbifold whose generic stabilizer is isomorphic to the finite group
\Gamma_{2}
. Let
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}\xrightarrow{\pi_{{\mathcal{F}}_{2}}}{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
be a complex vector bundle such that the total space
{\mathcal{F}}_{2}
is an effective orbifold. Then the resolution (cf. Corollary
7.12
)

(\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\times\tau_{{\mathcal{F}}_{2}})^{-1}_{[\bm{\gamma}_{1}]\times[\bm{\gamma}_{2}]}(0)

agrees with
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{-1}(0)_{[\bm{\gamma}_{1}]}
, where
[\bm{\gamma}_{2}]
is the stable isotropy class represented by
(\Gamma_{2},\{0\},\{0\})
.

Proof.

By Corollary
7.12
, we know that
\pi_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{S}}_{1}\times\tau_{{\mathcal{F}}_{2}}
is FOP regular, and the generic part of its zero locus indexed by
[\bm{\gamma}_{1}]\times[\bm{\gamma}_{2}]
is isomorphic to the product of
{\mathcal{U}}_{2}
with the the part of
{\mathcal{S}}_{1}^{-1}(0)
indexed by
[\bm{\gamma}_{1}]
. The agreement of resolutions is a consequence of Lemma
7.11
and Lemma
7.10
.
∎

8.
From derived orbifolds to orbifolds

The goal of this section is to prove the results from the introduction which describe the relationship between derived and underived orbifold bordism.

8.1.
Complex (derived) orbifold bordism

Elaborating on the discussion from Section
5.1
, we recall the definition of complex derived orbifold bordism following
[
Par21
, Section 5]
and
[
BX22b
, Section 7]
. For the discussion of this section, let
(X,A)
be a pair of orbispaces.

Firt, we introduce the notion of derived orbifold with boundary
{\mathcal{D}}
over
(X,A)
: this consists of lifting the underlying orbifold
{\mathcal{U}}
to an orbifold
over

(X,A)
.

Definition 8.1
.

A pair
({\mathcal{D}},f)
and
({\mathcal{D}}^{\prime},f^{\prime})
of derived orbifolds over
(X,A)
, are
bordant
if there exist a derived orbifold with boundary
(\hat{\mathcal{U}},\hat{\mathcal{E}},\hat{\mathcal{S}})
such that:

(1)

\hat{\mathcal{U}}
is a bordism from
({\mathcal{U}},f)
to
({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime},f^{\prime})
, and

(2)

using the embedding from (1),
(\hat{\mathcal{E}},\hat{\mathcal{S}})
restricts to
({\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
and
({\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime})
respectively.

The
derived orbifold bordism group
of
(X,A)
is then defined to be the free abelian group generated by
({\mathcal{D}},f)
, with
{\mathcal{D}}
compact, over
(X,A)
modulo the equivalence relations generated by stabilization and bordism (the latter includes the notion of restriction to a neighbourhood of the zero-locus). The proof that this is a homology theory (i.e. that some version of the Mayer-Viteoris condition holds) is non-trivial, and is due to Pardon
[
Par21
]
.

As in the underived case, one can impose tangential structures on
{\mathcal{D}}
to define structured derived orbifold bordism groups. In particular, one can define stable complex structures on a derived orbifold as stable complex structures on the underlying orbifold together with a stable complex structure on the defining vector bundle. We shall however use the following more general notion, which ends up being equivalent after stabilization:

Definition 8.2
.

A
stable complex structure
on a derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
(with boundary) is an isomorphism class of:

(1)

a vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{U}}
, together with

(2)

a complex structure on
T{\mathcal{U}}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\oplus\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{k}
, for some integer
k
, and a complex structure on
{\mathcal{E}}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
.

The stable complex structures specified by
({\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{\prime},k_{1})
and
({\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{\prime},k_{2})
are isomorphic if there exist
complex
vector bundles
{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{\prime\prime},{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{\prime\prime}\to{\mathcal{U}}
such that we have isomorphisms of complex vector bundles

(8.1)

\displaystyle(T{\mathcal{U}}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}_{1}\oplus\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{1}})\oplus{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\cong(T{\mathcal{U}}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}_{2}\oplus\underline{\mathbb{R}}^{k_{2}})\oplus{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{\prime\prime}

(8.2)

\displaystyle({\mathcal{E}}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}_{1})\oplus{\mathcal{E}}_{1}^{\prime\prime}

\displaystyle\cong({\mathcal{E}}\oplus{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}_{2})\oplus{\mathcal{E}}_{2}^{\prime\prime}.

Returning to the discussion of Section
2.2
, the restriction of any stably complex derived orbifold admits a natural stable complex structure, so does any stabilization (this is a minor change from
[
Par21
,
BX22b
]
who only allow stabilisation by complex vector bundles, which is immaterial because any vector bundle can be further stabilised to a complex bundle). The bordism relation can be refined to incorporate stable complex structures. Namely, a choice of normal vector to the inclusion of a derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
in the boundary of a stable complex derived orbifold
(\hat{\mathcal{D}},F)=(\hat{\mathcal{U}},\hat{\mathcal{E}},\hat{\mathcal{S}},F)
induces a stable complex structure on
{\mathcal{D}}
, with respect to the restriction of the bundle
\hat{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
, with the normal direction identified with an additional
\underline{\mathbb{R}}
stabilisation factor.

Then we say that any stably complex pair
({\mathcal{D}},f)
and
({\mathcal{D}}^{\prime},f^{\prime})
over
(X,A)
are stably complex bordant if there exists a bordism
(\hat{\mathcal{D}},F)=(\hat{\mathcal{U}},\hat{\mathcal{E}},\hat{\mathcal{S}},F)
as above further satisfying:

•

the stable complex structure of the pair
(\hat{\mathcal{U}},\hat{\mathcal{E}})
restricts to the stable complex structures of
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
and
({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime})
for some choice of normal vector field.

Definition 8.3
.

The
complex derived orbifold bordism
group of a pair of orbispaces
(X,A)

(8.3)

d\Omega_{*}^{U}(X,A)

is the abelian group (with additive structure coming from disjoint union) generated by compact derived orbifolds with boundary over
(X,A)
, equipped with a stable complex structure, modulo the the equivalence relation generated by stabilization and stably complex bordisms. It is
graded
by the virtual dimension.

We remark that the stable complex bordism relation is actually an equivalence relation, as shown in
[
Par21
, Proposition 5.1]
. From
[
BX22b
, Proposition 7.23]
, we know that
d\Omega_{*}^{U}
actually defines a generalized homology theory.

8.2.
Constructing orbifolds from derived orbifolds

We now prove Theorem
1.18
, which, then completes the proof of Theorem
1.21
. As in Section
5
, we formulate all constructions in terms of normally complex orbifolds. The main difference from the constructions of that section is that many more choices are required for the algorithm we use:

ALGORITHM II: From derived orbifolds to orbifolds

Input:
Suppose we are given an effective normally complex derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}
: i.e. an effective normally complex orbifold
{\mathcal{U}}
possibly with boundary, a normally complex vector bundle
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
, and a continuous section
{\mathcal{S}}:{\mathcal{U}}\to{\mathcal{E}}
such that
|{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)|
is compact.

Step 1

Apply
[
BX22b
, Lemma 3.15, Lemma 3.20]
to equip
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
with a straightening in the sense of Definition
2.20
and Definition
2.22
.

Step 2

For a sufficiently small
\epsilon>0
, apply Proposition
2.26
and Proposition
7.9
to obtain a smooth section such that:

•

{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon}
is a regular FOP section over
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
of degree at most
d\geq d_{0}
, where
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}\subset{\mathcal{U}}
is an open suborbifold with
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)\subset{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
and
|{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}|
compact,

•

\|{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon}-{\mathcal{S}}\|_{C^{0}}<\epsilon
, and

•

|{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon}^{-1}(0)|
is compact.

Step 3

For each isomorphism class of stable isotropy types
[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]
, apply Proposition
7.23
, to obtain a compact normally complex orbifold
{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
whose generic stabilizer point is indexed by
[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]
, which is the resolution of singularities

(8.4)

{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}:=\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}.

Moreover,
{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
comes with a map to
{\mathcal{U}}
.
∎

We call the combination of the three steps above the
Perturbation-Resolution algorithm
.

Remark 8.4
.

(1)

As in Section
5
, we can easily extend the results stated for derived manifolds to the case of derived manifolds with boundary, in particular, Proposition
7.23
.

(2)

The following auxiliary choices are made in the perturbation-resolution algorithm:

•

restrictions and stabilizations which make the ambient orbifold effective and induce genuine (normal) complex structures from the stable version;

•

straightenings, in the form of a Riemannian metric on
{\mathcal{U}}
and a connection on
{\mathcal{E}}
;

•

a cut-off degree
d
in the construction of the FOP sections;

•

FOP sections which achieve regularity.

(3)

After making
{\mathcal{U}}
effective, if
T{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}
are both endowed with a genuine complex structure, it follows from the construction that the tangent bundle of
{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
is equipped with a stable complex structure.

Proof of Theorem
1.18
.

We first prove the following statement: given a compact derived orbifold (with boundary)
({\mathcal{D}},f)=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}},f)
over a pair of orbispaces
(X,A)
which represents a class in
d\Omega_{*}^{U}(X,A)
, for each isomorphism class of groups
\gamma
, the output of applying Algorithm II with input datum
({\mathcal{D}},f)
is independent up to cobordism of the representative and various auxiliary data as listed in Remark
8.4
(2). This relies on the following:

Claim

If
({\mathcal{D}},f)
and
({\mathcal{D}}^{\prime},f^{\prime})=({\mathcal{U}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime},{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime},f^{\prime})
are bordant via
(\hat{\mathcal{D}},F)=(\hat{\mathcal{U}},\hat{\mathcal{E}},\hat{\mathcal{S}},F)
such that

•

the bordism
\hat{\mathcal{U}}
is effective,

•

\hat{\mathcal{U}}
is normally complex and the restriction of the normal complex structure along the collars of
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
agrees with the normal complex structure on
({\mathcal{U}}\coprod{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime})\times[0,1)
, and

•

\hat{\mathcal{E}}
is normally complex and the restriction of the normal complex structure along the collars of
{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
agrees with the normal complex structure on the pullback of
{\mathcal{E}}\coprod{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}
on
({\mathcal{U}}\coprod{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime})\times[0,1)
;

then for any auxiliary choices, the outputs of Algorithm II

(8.5)

{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]},\quad\quad\quad{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}

are bordant over
(X,A)
via the composition of the blow-down map and
f
(or
f^{\prime}
).

The proof of this claim goes as follows.

(a)

Given the straightenings on
{\mathcal{E}}\to{\mathcal{U}}
and
{\mathcal{E}}^{\prime}\to{\mathcal{U}}^{\prime}
, using the collar
({\mathcal{D}}\coprod{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})\times[0,1)\hookrightarrow\hat{\mathcal{D}}
and isomorphism between
\hat{\mathcal{E}}|_{({\mathcal{D}}\coprod{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})\times[0,1)}
and the pullback of
{\mathcal{E}}\coprod{\mathcal{E}^{\prime}}
via the projection
({\mathcal{D}}\coprod{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})\times[0,1)\to{\mathcal{D}}\coprod{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
, we can apply the constructions in
[
BX22b
, Section 3.3, 3.4]
to equip
\hat{E}\to\hat{\mathcal{D}}
with a straightening, whose underlying Riemannian metric coincides with the product Riemannian metric on
({\mathcal{D}}\coprod{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})\times[0,1)
(the interval
[0,1)
is endowed with the flat
dt^{2}
metric), and the connection coincides with the pullback connection.

(b)

If the FOP perturbations
{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon}
on
{\mathcal{D}}
and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}^{\prime}
on
{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime}
are chosen using cut-off degrees
d
and
d^{\prime}
respectively, by shrinking
\epsilon
and
\epsilon^{\prime}
if necessary, from a relative form of Proposition
7.9
as stated in
[
BX22b
, Proposition 6.7]
, we can find
\hat{d}\geq\max\{d,d^{\prime}\}
,
\hat{\epsilon}>0
, and a regular FOP section
{\mathcal{S}}_{\hat{\epsilon}}:\hat{\mathcal{U}}\to\hat{\mathcal{E}}
of cut-off degree
\hat{d}
with respect to the straightenings chosen in (a) such that it is equal to the pullback of
{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon}\coprod{\mathcal{S}}_{\epsilon^{\prime}}^{\prime}
on the collar
({\mathcal{D}}\coprod{\mathcal{D}}^{\prime})\times[0,1)
. Note here we need to apply Lemma
7.7
to make sure that enlarging the degree preserves the regularity of the FOP section.

(c)

After making the choices in (a) and (b), we observe that the functoriality as explored in Proposition
7.23
(see Proposition
7.16
for the isotropy-free case) makes sure that
{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
and
{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
are bordant through the resolution
\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
, and we actually have a collar
({\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}\coprod{\mathcal{Z}}^{\prime orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]})\times[0,1)\hookrightarrow\hat{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
.

Given the claim and item (3) in Remark
8.4
, the only ambiguity that remains comes from restrictions and stabilizations. Indeed, just to remind the reader, different choices of straightenings and FOP sections on the same derived orbifold chart can be compared over the trivial bordism.

Note that taking restriction does not affect the output of Algorithm II because all the constructions only depend on the information over any arbitrarily small open suborbifold of
{\mathcal{U}}
containing the zero locus
{\mathcal{S}}^{-1}(0)
. As for the invariance under stabilization, Lemma
7.10
shows that the regularity of FOP sections is preserved under stabilization, then so does the orbifold resolution of the zero loci by Proposition
7.17
and Proposition
7.24
. Therefore, bordism invariance under stabilization is reduced to the case proven in the claim.

In order to prove the compatibility of this splitting with the module structure, we observe that the geometric orbifold bordism, as a homology theory, splits as the direct sum

(8.6)

\Omega^{U}=\bigoplus_{\gamma}\Omega^{U}_{\gamma},

where
\gamma
ranges over isomorphism classes of finite groups and
\Omega^{U}_{\gamma}
consists of bordism classes of orbifolds whose ambient orbifold has generic stabilizer group lies in the isomorphism class
\gamma
. This decomposition, the index set respects the multiplicative structure in the sense that the Cartesian product induces a map

(8.7)

\Omega^{U}_{\gamma}\times\Omega^{U}_{\gamma^{\prime}}\to\Omega^{U}_{\gamma\times\gamma^{\prime}}.

With this in mind, the natural transformation in Theorem
1.18
, is defined in the following way: given a derived orbifold
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
(we omit the map to the pair
(X,A)
for notational simplicity), we consider all the stable isotropy types of the form

(8.8)

[(\tilde{\Gamma},\{0\},\{0\})],

namely, isomorphism classes of
(\tilde{\Gamma},\tilde{V},\tilde{W})
where
\tilde{V}
is isomorphic to
\tilde{W}
. Then for such a
\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}=(\tilde{\Gamma},\{0\},\{0\})
, the bordism class
{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\tilde{\bm{\gamma}}]}
lands in
\Omega^{U}_{\tilde{\gamma}}
, where
\tilde{\gamma}
stands for the isomorphism class of the group
\tilde{\Gamma}
. Then we construct the transformation
{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}
to be the evident map defined by assembling such a construction over all such stable isotropy classes:

(8.9)

{\mathcal{Z}}:=\bigoplus{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\bm{\gamma}]}:d\Omega^{U}\to\bigoplus_{\gamma}\Omega^{U}_{\gamma}.

It remains to see that
{\mathcal{Z}}
does define a natural transformation of generalized homology theories which respect the module structure and splits the inclusion map
\Omega^{U}\to d\Omega^{U}
.

The functoriality follows from the fact that
{\mathcal{Z}}
is defined by pushing forward the bordism class produced from the above algorithm. Therefore, it suffices to show the following diagram commutes:

(8.10)

\textstyle{{d\Omega_{*+1}^{U}(X,A)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}}
\textstyle{{\Omega^{U}_{*+1}(X,A)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\partial}
\textstyle{{d\Omega_{*}^{U}(A)}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{{\mathcal{Z}}}
\textstyle{\Omega_{*}^{U}(A).}

Consider a stably complex derived orbifold chart with boundary
{\mathcal{D}}=({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}},{\mathcal{S}})
with a map
f:({\mathcal{U}},\partial{\mathcal{U}})\to(X,A)
. Assume that there is an embedding
\partial{\mathcal{U}}\times[0,1)\rightarrow{\mathcal{U}}
. After choosing a straightening on
(\partial{\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}}|_{\partial{\mathcal{U}}})
, we can extend it to a straightening on
({\mathcal{U}},{\mathcal{E}})
which coincides with the product structure on the collar
\partial{\mathcal{U}}\times[0,1)
. After choosing a regular FOP section
{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}|_{\partial{\mathcal{U}}}
of
{\mathcal{S}}|_{\partial{\mathcal{U}}}
, we can extend it to an FOP perturbation
\tilde{\mathcal{S}}^{\prime}
of
{\mathcal{S}}
. The rest of the argument goes exactly like the discussion of bordisms.

For the module map property, given a bordism class in the summand
\Omega^{U}_{\gamma}
and a class in
d\Omega^{U}
, we can appeal to Proposition
7.25
to see that applying
{\mathcal{Z}}^{orb}_{[\bm{\gamma}]}
commutes with taking Cartesian product with bordism classes in
\Omega^{U}_{\gamma}
. For the splitting property, this follows from the observation that the tautological section of a stabilization of an orbifold by a faithful vector bundle, which represents the image of a geometric bordism class inside derived bordism, is FOP regular and the resolution does nothing to its zero locus.
∎

8.3.
Homotopical bordism

We now turn attention to our results comparing geometric and homotopical bordism. Following tom Dieck
[
tD70
]
, the standard formulation of the stably complex homotopical bordism of a
\Gamma
-pair
(X,A)
is in terms of the stably complex bordism of the product with
(X,A)
with the pair
(W,W\setminus{0})
associated to a complex
\Gamma
-representation
W
. An inclusion
W\to W^{\prime}
of such representations induces, by taking the product with the disc bundle of the orthogonal complement (and rounding corners), a map

(8.11)

\Omega^{U,\Gamma}_{*}((X,A)\times(W,W\setminus{0}))\to\Omega^{U,\Gamma}_{*+\dim W^{\prime}/W}((X,A)\times(W^{\prime},W^{\prime}\setminus{0})).

Definition 8.5
.

The homotopical bordism group is the direct limit

(8.12)

h\Omega^{U,\Gamma}(X,A)\equiv\lim_{W}\Omega^{U,\Gamma}_{*-\dim W}((X,A)\times(W,W\setminus{0}))

taken over all inclusions of complex finite-dimensional sub-representations of a universal
\Gamma
-representation.

An alternate definition is that of derived
\Gamma
-manifolds. This consists of a
\Gamma
-manifold
M
, a
\Gamma
-vector bundle
E
, and an equivariant section
s
of
E
. When
s^{-1}(0)
is compact, we say that a derived
\Gamma
-manifold is proper. We can specialize the discussion of Section
8.1
to obtain a notion of stably complex derived
\Gamma
-manifold. The derived stably complex bordism group

(8.13)

d\Omega^{U,\Gamma}(X,A)

is then generated by proper derived
\Gamma
-manifolds equipped with an equivariant map
(M,\partial M)\to(X,A)
. The following is a standard consequence of the fact that vector bundles can be stabilized to representation (compatibly with complex structures).

Proposition 8.6
.

Homotopical and derived bordism are naturally isomorphic homology theories of
\Gamma
-spaces.

Sketch of proof.

It is convenient for the proof to remove the requirement that the representative of a bordism class of the pair
(X,A)\times(W,W\setminus{0})
be compact with boundary. There is a reformulation of this bordism group as a bordism group of stably almost complex manifolds

(8.14)

(M,\partial M)\to\left(X\times W,A\times W\right)

whose projection to
W
is proper in a neighbourhood of the origin, and the comparison maps are given in one direction by passing to the interior of the manifold, and in the other by replacing
M
by the inverse image of a small ball in
W
.

Proceeding with this model for homotopical bordism, we obtain a tautological map from homotopical to derived bordism by considering
W
as a vector bundle over
M
, and the projection to
W
as a section. The properness conditions on the two sides match up, as do the equivalence relations of bordism and stabilization. In the other direction, we stabilise a derived orbifold so that its total space
\mathcal{U}
and the vector bundle
\mathcal{E}
are both stably complex, then stabilize further (by a complex complement for
\mathcal{E})
, so that
\mathcal{E}
is obtained from a
\Gamma
-representation.
∎

Proof of Theorem
1.7
:

For this statement, our goal is to construct a map

(8.15)

\displaystyle d\Omega^{U,\Gamma}_{*}(X,A)

\displaystyle\to\Omega^{U,\Gamma}_{*}(X,A)

\displaystyle(M,E,S)

\displaystyle\mapsto\tilde{Z}

which splits the inclusion of
\Gamma
-manifolds in derived
\Gamma
-manifolds. The right hand side decomposes as a direct sum indexed by the conjugacy classes of subgroups of
\Gamma
, corresponding to the generic stabilizer group.

The proof is exactly the same as that of Theorem
1.18
, appealing directly to Proposition
7.15
in the isotropy-free part (instead of Proposition
7.16
), and to Proposition
7.22
in general, and splitting according to conjugacy classes of subgroups (and virtual representations) rather than abstract isomorphism classes: given a
\Gamma
-equivariant section
S
of a
\Gamma
-vector bundle
E
over an effective
\Gamma
-manifold
M
, the result of our construction is the union, over all subgroups of
\Gamma
of the resolution algorithm applied to the intersection of a nearby FOP-regular section with the subset of the fixed point locus for groups in this conjugacy class, with the property that the normal representation and the obstruction representation are isomorphic (cf. the discussion after Equation (
8.8
)). The statement that this construction is compatible with the module structures follows from the compatibility of Algorithm II with taking products with manifolds.
∎

We complete this section with the proof of the last remaining result from Section
1.1
:

Proof of Theorem
1.10
.

For the statement arising from an injection
\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
of groups, we have to show that the induction functor which takes a
\Gamma
-manifold
M
to
M\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\prime}
, a
\Gamma
-vector bundle
E
to the induced bundle
E\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\prime}
, and acts on sections in the evident way is compatible with the perturbation-resolution algorithm. To establish this, we observe that the choices of
\Gamma
-equivariant straightenings of
M
and
E
that are required for the construction induce
\Gamma^{\prime}
-equivariant straightenings: indeed, we have a
\Gamma
-equivariant inclusion map
M\to M\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma^{\prime}
, and every non-empty fixed point stratum
\left(M\times\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{\Sigma^{\prime}}
for a subgroup
\Sigma^{\prime}
of
\Gamma
is the image of a fixed point stratum
M^{\Sigma}
for a subgroup
\Sigma
of
\Gamma
, under an element of
\Gamma^{\prime}
which is unique up to left multiplication by appropriate elements of
\Gamma
, so that the choice made near
M^{\Sigma}
freely induces such a choice for
\left(M\times\Gamma^{\prime}\right)^{\Sigma^{\prime}}
(in the sense that no additional conditions are imposed). Tracing through the construction then shows that, using at every step the induced choices yields a diffeomorphism of
\Gamma^{\prime}
-manifold between
\tilde{Z}\times_{\Gamma}\Gamma
(with
\tilde{Z}
as in Equation (
8.15
)) and the result of applying the algorithm to the induced group.

In the case of a surjection
\Gamma\to\Gamma^{\prime}
of groups, the argument is even more straightforward: a
\Gamma^{\prime}
-manifold gives rise to a
\Gamma
-manifold via the group homomorphism, and so are all the additional structures (of a vector, of a section, and of straightenings) in an evident way. The perturbation-resolution algorithm for
\Gamma^{\prime}
derived manifolds imposes more constraints (arising from conjugacy in
\Gamma^{\prime}
), but these nonetheless restrict to data of the required type for the corresponding
\Gamma
-manifold, yielding the compatibility of resolutions.
∎

9.
Applications

9.1.
Complex bordism valued Gromov–Witten invariants

Let
(X,\omega)
be a closed symplectic manifold and denote by
{\mathcal{J}}(X,\omega)
the space of
\omega
-tame almost complex structures on
TX
. For integers
g,k\geq 0
, a homology class
A\in H_{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})
, and
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(X,\omega)
, write

(9.1)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}(X,J,A)

for the moduli space of stable genus
g

J
-holomorphic maps, representing the homology class
A
, equipped with
k
marked points. It is a compact Hausdorff space with respect to the Gromov topology. The stabilization map to the Deligne–Mumford space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}
(we just use the coarse space here) and the evaluation maps at the marked points define a continuous map

(9.2)

\mathrm{st}\times\mathrm{ev}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}(X,J,A)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k}.

We explain how to extract a complex bordism class

(9.3)

\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{g.k}(X,A)

over
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k}
from the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}(X,J,A)
which is independent of
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(X,\omega)
, which thus gives rise to a
complex bordism valued Gromov–Witten invariant
.

9.1.1.
Global Kuranishi charts

A
global Kuranishi chart
for a compact Hausdorff topological space
Z
is a tuple

(9.4)

(G,M,E,\mathfrak{s},\Psi)

such that
G
is a compact Lie group,
M
is a topological
G
-manifold on which
G
acts almost freely,
E\to M
is a
G
-equivariant vector bundle,
\mathfrak{s}:M\to E
is a
G
-equivariant section, and
\Psi:\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0)/G\xrightarrow{\sim}Z
is a homeomorphism. We usually omit
\Psi
for the notation. We can introduce the following operations on global Kuranishi charts.

(1)

Shrinking
: if
M^{\prime}\subset M
is a
G
-invariant open subset containing
\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0)
, then

(9.5)

(G,M^{\prime},E|_{M^{\prime}},\mathfrak{s}|_{M^{\prime}})

also defines a global Kuranishi chart for
Z
.

(2)

Stabilization
: if
\pi_{E^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to M
is a
G
-equivariant vector bundle and denote by
\tau_{E^{\prime}}:E^{\prime}\to\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}E
the tautological section, then

(9.6)

(G,E^{\prime},\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}E\oplus\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}E^{\prime},\pi_{E^{\prime}}^{*}\mathfrak{s}\oplus\tau_{E^{\prime}})

is a global Kuranishi chart for
Z
.

(3)

Group enlargement
: if
G^{\prime}
is another compact Lie group and
\pi_{P}:P\to M
is a principal
G^{\prime}
-bundle, then

(9.7)

(G\times G^{\prime},P,P\times_{M}E,\pi_{P}^{*}\mathfrak{s})

defines a global Kuranishi chart for
Z
.

Note that if
M
comes with a continuous
G
-invariant map
f:M\to Y
to a topological space
Y
, it induces
G
-invariant continuous maps from
M^{\prime}
,
E^{\prime}
, and
P
from above by composing with the inclusion map,
\pi_{E^{\prime}}
, and
\pi_{P}
respectively.

A pair of global Kuranishi charts
(G,M,E,\mathfrak{s})
and
(G,M^{\prime},E^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}^{\prime})
equipped with
G
-invariant continuous maps
f:M\to Y
,
f:M^{\prime}\to Y
, are
bordant
if there exists a tuple
(G,\hat{M},\hat{E},\hat{\mathfrak{s}},\hat{F})
such that
\hat{M}
is a topological
G
-manifold with boundary with
\partial\hat{M}=M\coprod M^{\prime}
, along which the
G
-equivariant vector bundle
\hat{E}\to\hat{M}
and the
G
-equivariant section
\hat{\mathfrak{s}}:\hat{M}\to\hat{E}
restricts to
E\coprod E^{\prime}
and
\mathfrak{s}\coprod\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}
; furthermore, the restriction of the
G
-invariant
F
along
\partial\hat{M}
is required to agree with
f\coprod f^{\prime}
.

Using this language, the main theorems of
[
AMS21
,
AMS23
]
and
[
HS22
]
can be summarized as follows.

Theorem 9.1
.

For any
g,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
,
A\in H_{2}(M;{\mathbb{Z}})
, and
J\in{\mathcal{J}}(X,\omega)
, the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}(X,J,A)
admits a global Kuranishi chart
(G,M,E,\mathfrak{s})
, that depends on auxiliary data, and which is equipped with a continuous map
f:M\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k}
which restricts to (
9.2
) over
\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0)
.

For different choices of auxiliary data, the global Kuranishi charts are related by shrinkings, stabilizations, and group enlargements. For any pair of choices of
J,J^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{J}}(X,\omega)
, there exist auxiliary data such that the induced global Kuranishi charts
(G,M,E,\mathfrak{s})
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}(X,J,A)
and
(G,M^{\prime},E^{\prime},\mathfrak{s}^{\prime})
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}(X,J^{\prime},A)
together with the maps
f,f^{\prime}
are bordant. ∎

9.1.2.
Smoothing and tangential structures

In fact, a global Kuranishi chart
(G,M,E,\mathfrak{s})
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}(X,J,A)
from Theorem
9.1
is endowed with extra structures. The ambient
G
-manifold
M
comes with a continuous map
\pi
to a smooth
G
-manifold
F
such that
\pi:M\to F
is a
G
-equivariant fiberwise submersion and a
C^{1}_{loc}
G-bundle
[
AMS21
, Section 4.4, 4.5]
. As a result,
\pi:M\to F
has a well-defined vertical tangent bundle
T^{v}M
. Additionally, the
C^{1}_{loc}

G
-bundle structure guarantees that the
G
-equivariant vector bundle

(9.8)

T^{v}M\oplus\pi^{*}TF

defines a
G
-equivariant
vector bundle lift
of the tangent microbundle of
M
. Accordingly, Lashof’s stable
G
-smoothing theory
[
Las79
]
can be applied to
M
to equip a stabilization of it with a smooth structure such that
G
acts smoothly. For such a smooth structure, the corresponding stabilization of the vector bundle
E\to M
is isomorphic to a smooth
G
-equivariant vector bundle, which can be seen through approximating the classifying map.

Geometrically, the
G
-manifold
M
is constructed from the moduli space of certain perturbed
J
-holomorphic curves mapping into
X
, the base
F
is a
G
-equivariant smooth quasi-projective variety parametrizing suitable classes of stable maps of the standard complex projective spaces. An index theoretic analysis shows that the real
G
-equivariant virtual vector bundle

(9.9)

(T^{v}M\oplus\mathfrak{g}\oplus\pi^{*}TF)\ominus E\cong TM\oplus\mathfrak{g}\ominus E

admits a
G
-equivariant
stable complex
structure
[
AMS21
, Section 6.8]
.

9.1.3.
The bordism-valued invariants

Suppose that we have a global Kuranishi chart
(G,M,E,\mathfrak{s})
satisfying the following assumptions:

•

M
is a smooth manifold on which
G
acts smoothly;

•

E\to M
is a smooth
G
-equivariant vector bundle and
\mathfrak{s}:M\to E
is a smooth section;

•

the virtual
G
-equivariant vector bundle
(TM\oplus\mathfrak{g})\ominus E
is stably complex.

Then we see that the
G
-quotient

(9.10)

(M//G,E//G,\mathfrak{s}^{G})

defines a stably complex (cf. Definition
8.2
) and compact derived orbifold. If
f:M\to Y
is a
G
-invariant continuous map to a topological space
Y
, then (
9.10
) and the induced map
f:M//G\to Y
give rise to an element of
d\Omega_{*}^{U}(Y)
. Combined with the facts recalled in Section
9.1.2
, Theorem
9.1
admits the following enhancement:

Theorem 9.2
.

After taking stabilization, any global Kuranishi chart
(G,M,E,\mathfrak{s})
for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}(X,J,A)
can be promoted to one for which
M
is a smooth
G
-manifold and
E\to M
is a smooth
G
-equivariant vector bundle.

Approximating
\mathfrak{s}
by a smooth
C^{0}
-close
G
-equivariant section
\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}
, the class in
d\Omega_{*}^{U}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k})
defined by the derived orbifold chart

(9.11)

(M//G,E//G,(\mathfrak{s}^{\prime})^{G})

coupled with the continuous map
M//G\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k}
induced from
f:M\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k}
is independent of the choice of auxiliary data in the construction of global charts and smoothing, the almost complex structure
J
, and the approximation
\mathfrak{s}^{\prime}
. ∎

The cobordism invariance of choosing differrent smooth approximations is manifest. The rest is a compact form of the results from
[
AMS23
, Corollary 4.60]
and
[
HS22
, Theorem 2.16]
. For a more detailed exposition, the reader could consult
[
BX22b
, Section 8]
.

Theorem 9.3
.

Given a closed symplectic manifold
(X,\omega)
, for
g,k\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{\geq 0}
and
A\in H_{2}(X;{\mathbb{Z}})
, there exists a complex bordism valued Gromov–Witten invariant

(9.12)

(\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{g.k}(X,A),\mathrm{st}\times\mathrm{ev})\in\Omega_{*}^{U}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k})

whose underlying complex manifold
\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{g.k}(X,A)
parametrizes a ‘resolution’ of the moduli space of stable pseudo-holomorphic maps of genus
g
with
k
marked points representing the class
A
.

Proof.

We simply apply the splitting map
{\mathcal{Z}}:d\Omega_{*}^{U}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k})\to\Omega_{*}^{U}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k})
in (
1.15
) to the class constructed in Theorem
9.2
. The bordism map
\mathrm{st}\times\mathrm{ev}
is just defined to be the composition of the blow-down map with the restriction of the bordism map on the ambient orbifold.
∎

Remark 9.4
.

(1)

The class (
9.12
) should be viewed as a virtual fundamental class of the moduli space
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}(X,J,A)
with value in
\Omega_{*}^{U}
. One can either take the intersection of it with other classes from
\Omega_{*}^{U}(\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,k}\times X^{k})
or look at, e.g., Chern numbers of the complex manifold
\overline{\mathbf{M}}_{g.k}(X,A)
to define numerical enumerative invariants.

(2)

It is an open problem that to what extent the Kontsevich–Manin axioms of ordinary Gromov–Witten invariants are met by the
\Omega_{*}^{U}
-valued Gromov–Witten invariants. We expect it to be a subtle problem, because the resolution algorithm in
[
ATW19
]
, which is adapted in Section
7
, fails to respect the Cartesian product. This is in contrast with the multiplicativity property of the strong transversality of FOP sections established in
[
BX22a
, Section 2]
.

9.2.
Hamiltonian fibrations over
S^{2}

This subsection is devoted to the proof of Theorem
1.16
. This shall require a little bit of mental gymnastics, as the construction of spectra from geometric bordism is not quite standardized in the literature, and we shall use the Thom spectrum
MU
for the rest of this section.

9.2.1.
Reformulation in terms of Hamiltonian fibrations

Via the family version of clutching construction over
S^{2}
, one associates to the sweepout map the (universal) Hamiltonian fibration over
S^{2}
with fiber
X
over
0\in S^{2}
, which is a fiber bundle

(9.13)

P\to\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(M,\omega)\times S^{2}.

Lemma 9.5
.

There is a bijection between the set of homotopy classes of null homotopies for (
1.11
), and maps of spectra

(9.14)

P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to X_{+}\wedge MU

so that the composite

(9.15)

\left(\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(M,\omega)\times X\right)_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to X_{+}\wedge MU

agrees with the projection map.

Proof.

The most efficient way to perform the comparison is to use parametrized spectra: using the trivialization of the fibers over
0
as a section, the fibration
P
determines a parametrized spectrum
P\barwedge{\mathbb{S}}
over
\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(M,\omega)\wedge S^{2}
. We claim that both data correspond to a map from
P\barwedge{\mathbb{S}}
to the trivial parametrized spectrum with fiber
X_{+}\wedge MU
, extending the identity map over the basepoint.

The fact that Equation (
9.14
) is equivalent to such a map of parametrized spectra is a standard adjunction statement
[
MS06
, Theorem 11.4.1]
: the spectrum
P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}
is equivalent to the “total spectrum” of the parametrized spectrum
P\barwedge{\mathbb{S}}
, and this procedure is (left) adjoint to the construction which assigns to the spectrum
X_{+}\wedge MU
the associated trivial spectrum over
\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(M,\omega)\wedge S^{2}
. Our condition about the basepoint corresponds to the requirement that the restriction to
\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(M,\omega)\times X
agree with projection.

For the other equivalence, we use the fact that a parametrized spectrum over a base
B
is equivalent to a module spectrum over the suspension spectrum of the based loop space
\Omega B
; the standard reference for this fact has become
[
ABG18
]
, though the idea goes back at least to Waldhausen
[
Wal85
, Section 2.1]
. By the James construction, the based loop space of
\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(M,\omega)\wedge S^{2}
is a free algebra on
\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(M,\omega)\wedge S^{1}
, so that such a module spectrum is determined by the map in Equation (
1.11
). A null-homotopy of this map exactly corresponds to a module spectrum over
\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(M,\omega)\wedge S^{1}\wedge[0,1]_{+}
, which is trivial over
1
, and agrees with
P\barwedge{\mathbb{S}}
over
0
. Because the projection from
[0,1]
to either endpoint is a homotopy equivalence, we conclude that such a null-homotopy gives rise to the desired equivalence of parametrized spectra.
∎

9.2.2.
Some Pontryagin–Thom theory

Given a topological space
X
, the Pontryagin-Thom construction implies that the cohomology theory associated to the spectrum
X_{+}\wedge MU
is given, on smooth manifolds
Y
, by the geometric bordism theory of manifolds

(9.16)

N\to Y\times X

which are proper over
Y
, and are stably complex relative
TY
, in the sense that they are equipped with a complex vector bundle
I
, and an isoorphism

(9.17)

TN\oplus{\mathbb{R}}^{k}\cong I\oplus TY

for some integer
k
. This determines the cohomology theory on spaces having the homotopy type of a finite CW complex. We can extend this to general CW complex by a standard strategy that bypasses the fact that we cannot define the cohomology of a space as an inverse limit of the cohomology of subcomplexes because of the possible presence of
\lim^{1}
terms (c.f.
[
Mil95
]
): each such space may be represented as an increasing union
Y=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits_{i}Y_{i}
with
Y_{i}
smooth manifolds equipped with smooth embeddings
Y_{i}\to Y_{i+1}
. In this case, the cohomology theory associated to
X_{+}\wedge MU
, evaluation on
Y
, is given by bordism classes of the following data for each
i
:

(1)

A manifold
N_{i}\to Y_{i}\times X
which is proper over
Y_{i}
, equipped with a stable complex structure relative
TY_{i}
, and which is transverse to
Y_{i-1}
.

(2)

A cobordism
N_{i,i+1}
over
Y_{i}\times X
between
N_{i}
and the fibre product
N_{i+1}\times_{Y_{i}}Y_{i+1}
, which is also proper over
Y_{i}
, and equipped with a stable complex structure relative
TY_{i}
extending the stable complex structure on the two boundary components.

In the bordism relation between such data, we impose as usual properness, and include the data of stable complex structures. A completely analogous description applies to other bordism theories, including framed bordism, as we shall presently use:

Lemma 9.6
.

Let
P
be a smooth manifold. A manifold
M\to P\times X
whose projection to
P
is proper and which is equipped with a stable almost complex structure relative
TP

induces a morphism of spectra

(9.18)

P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to X_{+}\wedge MU.

Proof.

Since maps of spectra are determined by maps of the corresponding cohomology theories, let
Y
be a topological space with the homotopy type of a CW complex which we present, as discussed above, as an increasing union of manifolds
Y_{i}
. The cohomology theory associated to
P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}
, when evaluated on
Y
, is given by bordism classes of collections
\{(N_{i},N_{i-1,i})\}
of manifolds mapping to
P\times Y_{i}
, which are proper over
Y_{i}
, and stably framed relative
TY_{i}
