. Up to bordism, we map require that all such manifolds be transverse to
M
, under the evaluation map to
P
. This implies that the fibre products
\{(N_{i}\times_{P}M,N_{i-1,i}\times_{P}M)\}
are manifolds, which are again proper over
Y_{i}
(because
M
is proper over
P
), and are equipped with stable complex structures arising from the stable complex structure on
M
relative
TP
. The fibre products rae equipped with evaluation maps to
X
, and hence represents cobordism spaces for the cohomology theory associated to
X_{+}\wedge MU
. It is easy to see that this map depends only on the bordism class, as any bordism over
P
may be assumed to be transverse to
M
.

To prove that this construction defines a natural transformation, it is convenient to restrict attention to filtrations with the property that the
k
-skeleton of
Y
factors through
Y_{k_{i}}
for some integer
k_{i}
. Given any a map
Y^{\prime}\to Y
we may assume, possibly after passing to a subsequence, and reindexing, that
Y^{\prime}_{i}
maps by a smooth map to
Y_{i}
, commuting with the inclusions on both towers (this can be done inductively on
i
). Choosing the pairs
\{(N_{i},N_{i-1,i})\}
representing a cobordism on
Y
to be transverse to these maps, the fibre product defines a cobordism class on
Y^{\prime}
.
∎

Using the exact same method to a filtration
P=\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits P_{i}
of a topological space by smooth manifolds, we conclude:

Corollary 9.7
.

The data of a collection
\{(M_{i},M_{i,i+1})\}
of smooth manifolds over
P_{i}\times X
, which are proper over
P_{i}
, are equipped with stable complex structures relative
TP_{i}
, and such that
M_{i,i+1}
is a cobordism from
M_{i}
to
M_{i+1}\times_{P_{i}}P_{i+1}
determines a morphism of spectra

(9.19)

P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to X_{+}\wedge MU.

∎

Let us assume further that
X
is a smooth manifold, and that we are given a map

(9.20)

X\times B\to P

for some smooth manifold
B
.

Lemma 9.8
.

Let
M
be manifold over
P\times X
as in Lemma
9.6
, so that the projection map
M\to P
is transverse to
X\times B
. If the fiber product
M\times_{P}(X\times B)
agrees with the product of
B
with diagonal in
X
, then the composite

(9.21)

(X\times B)_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to X_{+}\wedge MU

is given by the projection
X\times B\to X
and the inclusion
{\mathbb{S}}\to MU
.

Proof.

We only discuss the map on homotopy groups, as the lift to the map of cohomology theories follows by modifying the argument below in exactly the same way as the proof of Lemma
9.6
. The map
(X\times B)_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}
corresponds, on homotopy groups, to assigning to a stably framed manifold over
X\times B
its image under the inclusion in
P
. The image of a stably framed manifold over
X\times B
after composition with the map
P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to X_{+}\wedge MU
is given by fibre product as in Lemma
9.6
. Under the assumption that the fiber product
M\times_{P}(X\times B)
agrees with the product of
B
with diagonal in
X
, the composite is simply the image under the projection map to
X
, considered as a stably complex manifold. This exactly corresponds to the map of spectra
(X\times B)_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to X_{+}\wedge MU
.
∎

Assuming now that
B
is a topological space, filtered by manifolds
B_{i}
, together with inclusions
X\times B_{i}\to P_{i}
commuting with the maps
P_{i}\to P_{i+1}
, we may extend the above result as follows:

Corollary 9.9
.

Let
\{(M_{i},M_{i,i+1})\}
be manifolds over
P\times X
as in Corollary
9.7
, so that the projections maps from
M_{i}
and
M_{i,i+1}
to
P_{i}
are transverse to
X\times B_{i}
. If the fiber product
M_{i}\times_{P_{i}}(X\times B_{i})
agrees with the product of
B_{i}
with diagonal in
X_{i}
, and the fiber product
M_{i,i+1}\times_{P_{i}}(X\times B_{i})
is the trivial cobordism, then the composite

(9.22)

(X\times B)_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to P_{+}\wedge{\mathbb{S}}\to X_{+}\wedge MU

is given by the projection
X\times B\to X
and the inclusion
{\mathbb{S}}\to MU
. ∎

9.2.3.
The topological part of the proof

Consider a
k
-dimensional stably almost complex compact manifold
B
with boundary, equipped with a smooth map to
\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(X,\omega)
. We write
P_{B}
for the pullback of
P
to
B
under this map. As explained in, e.g.,
[
Sav08
, Section 2]
, the following holds:

(1)

If
b\in B
corresponds to a Hamiltonian loop
\phi_{b}=\phi_{b}(t)\in\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(X,\omega)
, the fiber of
P_{B}
over
b
is given by a symplectic manifold
(P_{\phi_{b}},\omega_{b})
, which is the clutching construction over
S^{2}
using
\phi_{b}
. For any point in
S^{2}
, the symplectic connection of the Hamiltonian fibration
P_{\phi_{b}}\to S^{2}
identifies the horizontal symplectic vector space with the tangent space of
S^{2}
equipped with the symplectic form induced from the standard area form on
S^{2}
.

(2)

There exists a globally defined closed
2
-form
\Omega_{B}
on
P_{B}
such that for any
b\in B
, the restriction
\Omega_{B}|_{P_{\phi_{b}}}
agrees with
\omega_{b}
.

(3)

The structure group of
P_{B}\to B
is given by the group of bundle maps over
S^{2}
which act on the fibers by Hamiltonian diffeomorphisms and cover the identity map on
S^{2}
. Furthermore, such bundle maps can be assumed to be the identity near the fibers over
0,\infty\in S^{2}
.

The space of choices of the
2
-form
\Omega_{B}
is weakly contractible. For each fiber
P_{\phi_{b}}
, there is also an affine space parametrizing the space of symplectic forms
\omega_{b}
satisfying the prescribed condition. The choices here do not affect our discussion, except for an intermediate step in the construction of global Kuranishi charts, which will be implemented in Section
9.2.4
.

Let
S
be the one-point blow-up of
\mathbb{CP}^{1}\times\mathbb{CP}^{1}
. It admits a ruling
S\to\mathbb{CP}^{1}
with a unique reducible fiber
\mathbb{CP}^{1}\vee\mathbb{CP}^{1}
over
0
and in particular a smooth fiber over
\infty
. Taking the product with
B
, we have a smooth map

(9.23)

\pi_{B}:\tilde{S}=S\times B\to\mathbb{CP}^{1}\times B

with reducible fibers
\mathbb{CP}^{1}\vee\mathbb{CP}^{1}
over
\{0\}\times B
, and we have a family of smooth fibers over
\{\infty\}\times B
.

Lemma 9.10
.

There is a fibration

(9.24)

\pi_{\tilde{S}}:\tilde{P}_{B}\to\tilde{S}

where
\tilde{P}_{B}
admits a closed
2
-form
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}
satisfying the following properties:

(1)

the restriction of
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}
to each fiber is symplectomorphic to
(X,\omega)
;

(2)

there exists an open neighborhood
W_{\infty}
of
\infty\in\mathbb{CP}^{1}
such that the fibration
\tilde{P}_{B}|_{\pi_{B}^{-1}(W_{\infty}\times B)}
with the
2
-form
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}
is isomorphic to the trivial fibration

(9.25)

(W_{\infty}\times B\times S^{2}\times X,\omega_{S^{2}}|_{W_{\infty}}\oplus 0\oplus\omega_{S^{2}}\oplus\omega)

with projection
W_{\infty}\times B\times S^{2}\times X\to W_{\infty}\times B\times S^{2}
, where
\omega_{S^{2}}
is the standard area form on
S^{2}
.

(3)

for any
b\in B
, the space
\tilde{P}_{B}|_{\pi_{B}^{-1}(\{0\}\times\{b\})}\to\mathbb{CP}^{1}\vee\mathbb{CP}^{1}
is isomorphic to
P_{\phi_{b}}\vee P_{\phi_{b}^{-1}}\to\mathbb{CP}^{1}\vee\mathbb{CP}^{1}
, where each component of the reducible fiber carries the canonical defomation class of symplectic structures.

Proof.

This is a family version of
[
AMS21
, Lemma 3.3]
: if we compose (
9.24
) with the projection
\tilde{S}\to B
, each fiber of this map recovers in construction in
loc. cit.
. The existence of the closed
2
-form
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}
follows easily from a patching argument as exhibited in
[
Sav08
, Section 2.2]
.
∎

Let
(S^{2}\times B)_{hor}
be a section of (
9.24
) such that for each
b\in B
, the image of
(0,b)
lies in the projection of
P_{\phi_{b}}\setminus P_{\phi_{b}^{-1}}
. Define
(S^{2}\times B\times X)_{hor}
to be the preimage of
(S^{2}\times B)_{hor}
under
\pi_{B}
. By suitably choosing the section, we can assume that
(S^{2}\times B\times X)_{hor}
together with the restriction of
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}
is isomorphic to the product
(S^{2}\times B\times X,\omega_{S^{2}}\oplus 0\oplus\omega)
. For
t\in S^{2}\times B
, denote by
X_{t}
the fiber of
(S^{2}\times B\times X)_{hor}\to B\times X
, and define
P_{t}
to be the preimage of
t
under
\pi_{B}\circ\pi_{\tilde{S}}
. For
t\notin\{0\}\times B
, the space
(P_{t},\tilde{\Omega}_{b}|_{P_{t}})
with the projection to
S^{2}
is isotopic as a Hamiltonian fibration to the product
(S^{2}\times X,\omega_{S^{2}}\oplus\omega)
, which is further canonical when
t\in W_{\infty}\times B
.

Consider the projection

(9.26)

p_{B}:\tilde{P}_{B}\xrightarrow{\pi_{\tilde{S}}}\tilde{S}\xrightarrow{\pi_{B}}\mathbb{CP}^{1}\times B\to B,

which is a smooth submersion such that the restriction of
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}
to each fiber defines a symplectic form. Based on the discussion in
[
Sav08
, Section 2.5.1]
, there exists a smooth family of almost complex structures
\{J_{b}\}_{b\in B}
, varying smoothly in
b\in B
, such that:

(1)

for each
b\in B
, the almost complex structure
J_{b}
is compatible with
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}|_{p_{B}^{-1}(b)}
;

(2)

endowing
S
with the integrable complex structure, the projection map
\tilde{P}_{B}|_{p_{B}^{-1}(b)}\to S
induced from
\pi_{B}
is pseudo-holomorphic;

(3)

over the region (
9.25
), for any
b\in B
, the endomorphism
J_{b}
is a product.

We use such a family of almost complex structures to construct suitable moduli spaces of
J
-holomorphic curves. For any
b\in B
, let
A
be the homology class represented by the sphere
\{\infty\}\times\{b\}\times S^{2}\times\{pt\}
in
W_{\infty}\times\{b\}\times S^{2}\times X
from (
9.25
). Consider the moduli space

(9.27)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A):=\{(b,u)\ |\ b\in B,u\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(p_{B}^{-1}(b),J_{b},A)\}

which comes with the evaluation map

(9.28)

\mathrm{ev}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A)\to\tilde{P}_{B}\times\tilde{P}_{B}.

The virtual dimension of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A)
is
k+2n+6
. Define the moduli space

(9.29)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{h}^{B}:=\mathrm{ev}^{-1}(\tilde{P}_{B}\times(S^{2}\times B\times X)_{hor})

which has virtual dimension
k+2n+4
. Over the open subset (
9.25
), we see that

(9.30)

(S^{2}\times B\times X)_{hor}\cap(W_{\infty}\times B\times S^{2}\times X)=W_{\infty}\times B\times X.

Then define the moduli space

(9.31)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{h}^{B}\supset\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{B}_{W_{\infty}}:=\mathrm{ev}^{-1}((W_{\infty}\times B\times S^{2}\times X)\times(W_{\infty}\times B\times X)).

Lemma 9.11
.

\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{B}_{W_{\infty}}
is regular, and the evaluation map defines a diffeomorphism onto the graph of the projection map

(9.32)

(W_{\infty}\times B\times S^{2}\times X)\to(W_{\infty}\times B\times X).

Proof.

Recall that for any
b\in B
, we have asked
J_{b}
to be of product form over (
9.25
). Therefore, the regularity follows directly from the automatic transversality of the Cauchy–Riemann operator associated with the degree
1
holomorphic map from
S^{2}
to itself. As there is exactly one such curve after imposing the evaluation constraint, we see that
\mathrm{ev}
defines a diffeomorphism onto to the manifold described above.
∎

Remark 9.12
.

According to our assumption,
B
has a stable complex structure. Therefore,
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{B}_{W_{\infty}}
has a stable complex structure because the symplectic structures on
S^{2}
and
X
induces a canonical class of stable complex structures on their tangent bundles. On the other hand, index theory equips
\overline{\mathcal{M}}^{B}_{W_{\infty}}
with a stable complex structure because the linearized Cauchy–Riemann operator associated to each element in the moduli space is homotopic to a complex linear Fredholm operator. The diffeomorphism
\mathrm{ev}
furthermore respects the stable complex structures.

We similarly introduce

(9.33)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}^{B}:=\mathrm{ev}^{-1}(\pi_{B}^{-1}(\{\infty\}\times B)\times(S^{2}\times B\times X)_{hor}).

Now we are ready to state the key geometric input from the theory of global charts
[
AMS21
]
of moduli spaces, whose proof is discussed in the next subsection.

Theorem 9.13
.

There exists a stably complex derived orbifold

(9.34)

{\mathcal{D}}_{B}=({\mathcal{U}}_{B},{\mathcal{E}}_{B},{\mathcal{S}}_{B}),

whose section section is
{\mathcal{S}}_{B}
continuous
2
2

2

We require the section to be smooth in the definition of derived orbifold charts, so hopefully emphasizing the continuity here would not cause any confusion.
,
together with a map

(9.35)

\widetilde{\mathrm{ev}}:{\mathcal{U}}_{B}\to\tilde{P}_{B}\times_{B}\tilde{P}_{B}

satisfying the following properties.

(1)

{\mathcal{U}}_{B}
is an effective orbifold, and there exists a homeomorphism
{\mathcal{S}}_{B}^{-1}(0)\cong\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A)
.

(2)

The restriction of
\widetilde{\mathrm{ev}}
along
{\mathcal{S}}_{B}^{-1}(0)
agrees with
\mathrm{ev}:\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A)\to\tilde{P}_{B}\times_{B}\tilde{P}_{B}
.

(3)

The projection map to either factor
\tilde{P}_{B}
is proper.

Corollary 9.14
.

The derived orbifold chart
{\mathcal{D}}_{h}=({\mathcal{U}}_{h},{\mathcal{E}}_{h},{\mathcal{S}}_{h})
induced from the Cartesian square

(9.36)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}}_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}}_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{P}_{B}\times_{B}(S^{2}\times B\times X)_{hor}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{P}_{B}\times_{B}\tilde{P}_{B}}

inherits a stable complex structure and
{\mathcal{S}}_{h}^{-1}(0)
is homeomorphic to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{h}^{B}
.

Similarly, the derived orbifold chart
{\mathcal{D}}_{\infty}=({\mathcal{U}}_{\infty},{\mathcal{E}}_{\infty},{\mathcal{S}}_{\infty})
specified by the fiber product diagram

(9.37)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}}_{\infty}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{U}}_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{B}^{-1}(\{\infty\}\times B)\times_{B}(S^{2}\times B\times X)_{hor}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{P}_{B}\times_{B}\tilde{P}_{B}}

is stably complex, and
{\mathcal{S}}_{\infty}^{-1}(0)
is homeomorphic to
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{\infty}^{B}
.

Proof.

This is a direct consequence of Lemma
9.11
and Theorem
9.13
. ∎

By applying the perturbation-resolution algorithm to the derived orbifold chart
{\mathcal{D}}_{h}
, we obtain a stably almost complex manifold

(9.38)

\overline{\mathbf{M}}^{B}_{h}.

The composition of
\widetilde{\mathrm{ev}}
with the natural map
\overline{\mathbf{M}}^{B}_{h}\to{\mathcal{U}}_{h}
produces a pair of maps

(9.39)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\overline{\mathbf{M}}^{B}_{h}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\tilde{P}_{B}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(S^{2}\times B\times X)_{hor}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{ev}_{2}}

We compose with the projection from
S^{2}\times B\times X
to
X
, to obtain a manifold which is proper over
\tilde{P}_{B}
.Consider the inclusion
X\times B\to\tilde{P}_{B}
associated to the point
(\infty,\infty)
in
\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}\times\mathbb{C}\mathbb{P}^{1}
. According to Lemma
9.11
, the evaluation map from
\overline{\mathbf{M}}^{B}_{h}
to
\tilde{P}_{B}
is transverse to this submanifold, and the associated submanifold of
X\times B\times X
is therefore the product of
B
with the diagonal.

Moreover, given an inclusion
B\to B^{\prime}
of bases, the perturbation-resolution algorithm yield a cobordism of almost complex manifold

(9.40)

\overline{\mathbf{M}}^{B,B^{\prime}}_{h}.

between
\mathbf{M}^{B}_{h}
and the fibre product of
\mathbf{M}^{B^{\prime}}_{h}
with the inclusion of
\tilde{P}_{B^{\prime}}
in
\tilde{P}_{B^{\prime}}
. Indeed, the space of data for defining the derived orbifold
{\mathcal{D}}_{B}
is contractible, and any choice of data for defining
{\mathcal{D}}_{B^{\prime}}
thus pulls back to define a derived orbifold which is cobordant to
{\mathcal{D}}_{B}
after stabilization. The claim then follows form the compatibility of the perturbation-resolution algorithm with cobordisms and stabilizations. This cobordism will again have the property that its fibre product with
X\times B
gives the product of
B\times I
with the diagonal of
X
.

We can now complete the proof of our application, modulo the geometric construction of parametric families of derived orbifolds:

Proof of Proposition
1.16
.

Choose a sequence
B_{1}\to B_{2}\to\cdots
of embeddings of compact stably almost manifolds with boundary, lying over
\Omega\mathrm{Ham}(M,\omega)\times S^{2}
, with the property that the map from
\mathop{\mathchoice{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\displaystyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\textstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}{\vtop{\halign{#\cr\hfil$\scriptscriptstyle\operator@font colim$\hfil\cr\kern 1.5pt\cr\rightarrow\textstyle\cr\kern-1.0pt\cr\cr}}}}\displaylimits B_{i}
is a homotopy equivalence. Given the preceding discussion, the result now follows from Corollary
9.9
.

∎

9.2.4.
Global Kuranishi charts for families

We move on to explain the constructions in Theorem
9.13
. It is almost the same as the original construction described in
[
AMS21
, Section 6]
, so we will be brief and refer the reader to
loc. cit.
for more detailed arguments. As noted in
[
AMS21
, Section 6.9]
, the global Kuranishi chart for a moduli space of
J
-holomorphic curves with marked points can be obtained by pulling back such a chart for a moduli space of
J
-holomorphic curves without marked points via the forgetful map. Therefore, we will focus on constructing global Kuranishi charts for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,0}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A)
, which is the unmarked version of (
9.27
).

Lemma 9.15
.

There exists a closed
2
-form
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}
defined on
\tilde{P}_{B}
such that:

(1)

the restriction of
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}
to each fiber of
p_{B}
from (
9.26
) defines a symplectic form which is tamed by
J_{b}
;

(2)

the cohomology class
[\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}|_{p_{B}^{-1}(b)}]
lies in the image of the natural inclusion map of cohomology groups
H^{2}(p_{B}^{-1}(b);{\mathbb{Z}})\to H^{2}(p_{B}^{-1}(b);{\mathbb{R}})
.

Proof.

For point
b\in B
, using the description from
[
Sav08
, Section 2.2]
, we see that the cohomology class of
[\tilde{\Omega}_{B}|_{p_{B}^{-1}(b)}]
is uniquely determined by the cohomology class of the symplectic form on the complex surface
S
and the class
[\omega]\in H^{2}(X;{\mathbb{R}})
. By choosing a sufficiently fine rational approximation and applying rescaling, we can find the desired
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}\in H^{2}(\tilde{P}_{B};{\mathbb{R}})
.
∎

From now on, let us fix:

(1)

a closed
2
-form
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}
as in Lemma
9.15
;

(2)

a positive integer
d:=\langle\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime},A\rangle
.

Denote by
\mathcal{F}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,0}(\mathbb{CP}^{d},d)
the Zariski open subset of the moduli space of genus
0
degree
d
holomorphic maps into
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
which consists of maps whose image is not contained in any linear subspace, and let
\mathcal{C}\to\mathcal{F}
be the universal curve. By
[
AMS21
, Lemma 6.4]
, both
\mathcal{F}
and
\mathcal{C}
are smooth complex quasi-projective varieties.

Using the integral
2
-form
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}
defined over
\tilde{P}_{B}
, for any pair
(b,u)\in\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,0}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A)
such that
u:\Sigma\to p^{-1}_{B}(b)
is a
J_{b}
-holomorphic map defined over a nodal genus
0
curve
\Sigma
, by
[
AMS21
, Lemma 6.8]
, there exists a holomorphic Hermitian line bundle
L_{u^{*}\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}}\to\Sigma
whose Chern connection has curvature
-2\pi iu^{*}\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}
, and it is uniquely defined up to isomorphism. Moreover, due to the positivity of
u^{*}\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}
, as implied by the fact that
\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}|_{p^{-1}_{B}(b)}
is tamed by
J_{b}
, we know that
H^{1}(\Sigma,L_{u^{*}\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}})=0
and
\dim_{\mathbb{C}}H^{0}(\Sigma,L_{u^{*}\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}})=d+1
. Upon choosing a basis
\{s_{0},\dots,s_{d}\}
of
H^{0}(\Sigma,L_{u^{*}\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}})
, the projectivization
[s_{0}:\cdots:s_{d}]
defines a holomorphic map
\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
, which actually lies in
\mathcal{F}\subset\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,0}(\mathbb{CP}^{d},d)
. This motivates the following definition.

Definition 9.16
.

A
framed genus
0
curve in
p_{B}:\tilde{P}_{B}\to B
is a quadruple
(b,u,\Sigma,F)
where

(1)

\Sigma
is a genus
0
nodal curve;

(2)

b\in B
, and
u\to p_{B}^{-1}(b)
is a smooth map in the class
A
such that
2
-form
u^{*}\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}
is positive over any unstable component of
\Sigma
;

(3)

F=(s_{0},\dots,s_{d})
forms a basis of
H^{0}(\Sigma,L_{u^{*}\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime}})
such that the Hermitian matrix

(9.41)

\mathcal{H}(b,u,\Sigma,F)=(\int_{\Sigma}\langle s_{i},s_{j}\rangle u^{*}\tilde{\Omega}_{B}^{\prime})_{i,j}

has positive eigenvalues.

The holomorphic map
\iota_{F}:=[s_{0}:\cdots:s_{d}]:\Sigma\to\mathbb{CP}^{d}
is called the
domain map
.

We can define the isomorphism relation between a pair of framed curves in the standard way.

Next, we choose

(1)

a Hermitian line bundle
\mathcal{L}\to\mathcal{C}
whose restriction to each fiber is ample and the
PU(d+1)
-action on
\mathcal{C}
induced from the
PGL(d+1)
-action on
\mathbb{CP}^{d}
is lifted to a
PU(d+1)
-action on
\mathcal{L}
which preserves the Hermitian structure;

(2)

a sufficiently large integer
k\gg 1
.

Definition 9.17
.

The
thickened moduli space

\mathcal{T}
(which is endowed with the Gromov topology after performing a graph construction) is the moduli space of tuples
(b,u,\Sigma,F,\eta)
for which:

(1)

(b,u,\Sigma,F)
is a framed genus
0
curve;

(2)

\eta\in H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\iota_{F}^{*}T\mathcal{C},u^{*}Tp_{B}^{-1}(b))\otimes\iota_{F}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{H^{0}(\iota_{F}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{k})}
;

(3)

The equation

(9.42)

\overline{\partial}_{J_{b}}u+\langle\eta\rangle\circ d\iota_{F}=0

holds where
\langle\eta\rangle
is the image of the Hermitian pairing

(9.43)

H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\iota_{F}^{*}T\mathcal{C},u^{*}Tp_{B}^{-1}(b))\otimes\iota_{F}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{H^{0}(\iota_{F}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{k})}\to C^{\infty}(\overline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\iota_{F}^{*}T\mathcal{C},u^{*}Tp_{B}^{-1}(b))).

The
obstruction bundle

\mathcal{E}
is defined to be the bundle over
\mathcal{T}
whose fiber over
(b,u,\Sigma,F,\eta)
is

(9.44)

H^{0}(\overline{\mathrm{Hom}}(\iota_{F}^{*}T\mathcal{C},u^{*}Tp_{B}^{-1}(b))\otimes\iota_{F}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{k})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\overline{H^{0}(\iota_{F}^{*}\mathcal{L}^{k})}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{d+1},

where
\mathcal{H}_{d+1}
is the space of
(d+1)\times(d+1)
Hermitian matrices.

The
Kuranishi map
is the section
\mathfrak{s}:\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{E}
defined by

(9.45)

\mathfrak{s}:(b,u,\Sigma,F,\eta)\mapsto(\eta,\exp^{-1}(\mathcal{H}(b,u,\Sigma,F))),

where
\exp^{-1}
is the inverse of the exponential map, which is well-defined over the space of positive definite Hermitian matrices.

If
k\gg 1
, the bundle
\mathcal{E}
is indeed a vector bundle by positivity and compactness of
B
. The thickened moduli space
\mathcal{T}
admits a continuous map

(9.46)

\displaystyle pr:\mathcal{T}

\displaystyle\to\mathcal{F}\times B

\displaystyle(b,u,\Sigma,F,\eta)

\displaystyle\mapsto(\iota_{F},b).

Introduce the notation
G=PU(d+1)
. Then
\mathcal{T}
admits a
G
-action and
\mathcal{E}
is a
G
-equivariant vector bundle. We summarize some salient features of
(G,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{s})
as follows.

Proposition 9.18
.

(1)

The induced topology on
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,0}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A)
as the zero locus
\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0)
agrees with the Gromov topology.

(2)

The
G
-action on
\mathcal{T}
has finite stabilizers, and the orders of stabilizers are uniformly bounded.

(3)

For
k
sufficiently large, the linearization of (
9.42
) is surjective over a (precompact) open neighborhood of
\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0)
. Therefore, up to a further shrinking,
(G,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{s})
defines a global Kuranishi chart for
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,0}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A)
.

(4)

The projection
pr:\mathcal{T}\to\mathcal{F}\times B
is a topological submersion which defines a
C^{1}_{loc}

G
-bundle structure in the sense of
[
AMS21
, Section 4.5]
.

(5)

Using the structure described in (4), the virtual
G
-equivariant vector bundle

(9.47)

T^{v}\mathcal{T}\oplus\mathfrak{g}\oplus pr^{*}T(\mathcal{F}\times B)-\mathcal{E}

defines a class in the
complex
G-equivariant
K
-theory.

Proof.

These are all proven in
[
AMS21
, Section 6]
. The compactness of
B
makes sure that the Hörmander techniques used there can also work in the family setting due to the openness of transversality. Moreover, because
TB
is stably complex, the complex orientation can be constructed. As for the
C^{1}_{loc}

G
-bundle property, the topological gluing construction in
[
Par16
, Appendix B]
can be done without difficulties in the family setting, whose counterpart in the setting of Hamiltonian Floer theory can be found in
[
Par16
, Appendix C]
. Accordingly, the arguments of
[
AMS21
, Theorem 6.1]
carry over here.
∎

Proof of Theorem
9.13
.

This follows from the proof of
[
AMS21
, Proposition 5.35]
, which shows that the Property (4) ensures that one can apply Lashof’s
G
-equivariant stable smoothing theory to equip a stabilization of the global Kuranishi chart
(G,\mathcal{T},\mathcal{E},\mathfrak{s})
with a smooth structure, except that the stabilized Kuranishi section may only be continuous. Pulling back such a global Kuranishi chart via the forgetful map

(9.48)

\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,2}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A)\to\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{0,0}(\tilde{P}_{B},\{J_{b}\},A),

[
AMS21
, Lemma 4.5]
shows that a further stabilization and shrinking can make sure that the evaluation map on the thickened moduli space becomes submersive. By taking the corresponding derived orbifold chart as in (
9.10
), we obtain
{\mathcal{D}}_{B}=({\mathcal{U}}_{B},{\mathcal{E}}_{B},{\mathcal{S}}_{B})
.
∎
